\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\hat{\varrho}\left(u;\nu-k,z\right)e^{-{\rm i}kz}\textnormal{d}z=\left.\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varrho}\left(u;j,z\right)\eta(z)e^{-{\rm i}\xi z}\textnormal{d}z\ \right|_{\left(j,\xi\right)=\left(\nu-k,k\right)}\,.

(2.44)

Therefore by (
2.41
), (
2.42
) and (
2.44
)
the operator
\mathcal{T}_{\varrho}
in (
2.40
) is equal to

\mathcal{T}_{\varrho}=\textnormal{Op}^{W}\left[a_{\psi}\left(u;x,\xi\right)\right]=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a\left(u;x,\xi\right)\right]+R\left(u\right)\qquad\text{where}\qquad R\left(u\right)\in\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{q}

(2.45)

and

a\left(u;x,\xi\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{a}\left(u;j,\xi\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,\quad\hat{a}\left(u;j,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varrho}\left(u;j,z\right)\eta(z)e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\,.

(2.46)

In order to prove the lemma, in view of (
2.45
),
it is sufficient to show that

a\left(u;x,\xi\right)
defined in (
2.46
) is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-\left(1+n\right)}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

according to
Definition

2.2
.
Notice that

a\left(u;x,\xi\right)
satisfies the reality condition (
2.20
).
Moreover, in view of the support properties of

\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)
and
\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)
, it results that

\hat{a}\left(u;j,\xi\right)\neq 0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\left|\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right|\sim\left|\xi\right|\,,\quad\left|\xi\right|\gtrsim 1\,,\quad\left|\xi\right|\sim\left\langle\xi\right\rangle\,.

(2.47)

We decompose the Kernel function

\varrho\left(u;x,z\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{F}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\qquad\text{as}\qquad\varrho\left(u;x,z\right)=\sum_{q=p}^{N}\varrho_{q}\left(u;x,z\right)+\varrho_{>N}\left(u;x,z\right)\,,

where

\varrho_{q}\left(u;x,z\right)
are homogenous Kernel functions in
\widetilde{K\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{q}
and

\varrho_{>N}\left(u;x,z\right)
is a non-homogenous Kernel function
in
K\mathcal{F}^{n}_{K,0,N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
. Accordingly we decompose the
symbol
a\left(u;x,\xi\right)
in (
2.46
) as

a\left(u;x,\xi\right)=\sum_{q=p}^{N}a_{q}\left(u;x,\xi\right)+a_{>N}\left(u;x,\xi\right)

where

\displaystyle a_{q}\left(u;x,\xi\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{a}_{q}\left(u;j,\xi\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{q}\left(u;j,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varrho}_{q}\left(u;j,z\right)\eta(z)e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\,,

(2.48)

\displaystyle a_{>N}\left(u;x,\xi\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{a}_{>N}\left(u;j,\xi\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{>N}\left(u;j,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varrho}_{>N}\left(u;j,z\right)\eta(z)e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\,.

We now prove that, according to
Definition

2.2
,

\displaystyle a_{q}\in

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{\Gamma}^{-\left(1+n\right)}_{q}\,,\quad\forall q=p,\ldots,N\,,

(2.49)

\displaystyle a_{>N}\in

\displaystyle\ \Gamma^{-\left(1+n\right)}_{K,0,N+1}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right].

(2.50)

Step 1

(Proof of (
2.49
))
.

In view of (
2.32
)
the
q
-homogeneous component
a_{q}(u;x,\xi)
in
(
2.48
) has an expansion as in
(
2.10
) (recall the notation
\vec{\jmath}_{q}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{q})
)

\displaystyle a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(\xi)=\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varrho_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}}(z)\,\eta(z)\,e^{-{\rm i}\xi z}\textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle j=j_{1}+\ldots+j_{q}\,.

Let us prove it satisfies (
2.11
) with
m=-(1+n)
.
Decomposing
1=\chi_{1}(\cdot)+\chi_{2}(\cdot)

where
\chi_{1}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
is a smooth cutoff function
supported and equal to
1
near
0
,
we decompose

a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(\xi)=a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}^{(1)}(\xi)+a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}^{(2)}(\xi)=\sum_{\mathsf{j}=1}^{2}\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{\mathsf{j}}\left(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle z\right)\varrho_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q}}(z)\,\eta(z)\,e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\,.

(2.51)

By (
2.33
) (with
l=0
)
and since
n>-1
we deduce

\left|a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}^{(1)}\left(\xi\right)\right|\lesssim\int_{\left|z\right|\lesssim\left.1\middle/\left\langle\xi\right\rangle\right.}\left|{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}\right|^{\mu}|z|^{n}\textnormal{d}z\lesssim\left|{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n\right)}\,.

(2.52)

We now estimate
a_{\vec{\jmath}}^{(2)}(\xi)
. From

e^{-{\rm i}z\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)}=\left[-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)\right]^{-l}\partial_{z}^{l}\Big(e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\Big)
for any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
we obtain, by an integration by parts (use (
2.43
) and that

\chi_{2}\left(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle z\right)
vanishes near zero), that

a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}^{(2)}(\xi)=\left[-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)\right]^{-l}\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3}=l}c_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\,Y_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}(z)\,\textnormal{d}z\\
\text{where}\quad Y_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}(z)\vcentcolon=\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{1}}\left(\partial_{z}^{l_{1}}\chi_{2}\right)\left(z\right)\,\partial_{z}^{l_{2}}\eta(z)\,\partial_{z}^{l_{3}}\varrho_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(z)\,.

(2.53)

Since
\varrho_{q}\left(u;x,z\right)
is a Kernel function
in
\widetilde{K\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{p}
, using (
2.33
)
and exploiting that
\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-1}\sim|z|

on the support of
\chi_{2}^{\left(l_{1}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle z\right)
for any
l_{1}\geq 1
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|Y_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}(z)\right|\textnormal{d}z\lesssim\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{1}}\ \int_{\frac{1}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\sim|z|}\left|z\right|^{n-l_{3}}\textnormal{d}z\lesssim\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{1}+l_{3}-\left(n+1\right)}.

(2.54)

When
l_{1}=0
we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|Y_{0,l_{2},l_{3}}(z)\right|\textnormal{d}z\lesssim\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\int_{\frac{\delta}{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\leq\left|z\right|\leq\frac{3\pi}{2}}\left|z\right|^{n-l_{3}}\textnormal{d}z\lesssim\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left(1+\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{3}-\left(n+1\right)}\right).

(2.55)

Then by (
2.53
), (
2.54
), (
2.55
)
and (
2.47
), we deduce, for
l>n+1
,

\big|a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}^{(2)}(\xi)\big|\lesssim_{l}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n\right)}\,.

(2.56)

The bounds (
2.52
) and (
2.56
) prove
that
a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(\xi)
in (
2.51
) satisfies
the estimate (
2.11
) (for
\beta=0
and
m=-(1+n)
).
Since, for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(\xi)=\sum_{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}=\beta}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}}\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varrho_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(z)\left(-{\rm i}z\right)^{\beta_{3}}e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\,,

(2.57)

using (
2.15
), (
2.47
), the fact that
\chi
is supported near
0
,
that

z^{\beta_{3}}\varrho_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(z)
satisfies (
2.33
)
with (
n
replaced by
n+\beta_{3}
, cf. Remark
2.29
)
and repeating the bound obtained for
\beta_{3}=0
for the integral term in
Eq.

2.57
, we obtain

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a_{{\vec{\jmath}}_{q}}(\xi)\right|\lesssim_{\beta}\sum_{\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}=\beta}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-{\beta_{1}}}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-{\beta_{2}}}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-{(1+n+\beta_{3})}}\lesssim_{\beta}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{q}\right|^{\mu}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-{(1+n+\beta)}}\,.

Note that actually for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\beta_{2}\geq 1
, the derivative

\partial_{\xi}^{{\beta}_{2}}\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)=0
,
for any
|\xi|\geq 2
.
This concludes the proof of (
2.49
).

Step 2

(Proof of (
2.50
))
.

We argue similarly to the previous step.
Recalling (
2.48
), for any
0\leq k\leq K
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
we decompose, with
\chi_{\mathsf{j}},\ \mathsf{j}=1,2
defined as in (
2.51
),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}a_{>N}\left(u;x,\xi\right)=\ I_{1}+I_{2}\qquad\text{where}

(2.58)

\displaystyle I_{\mathsf{j}}\vcentcolon=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\left\langle\xi\right\rangle\right)\widehat{\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\varrho}_{>N}\left(u;j,z\right)\eta\left(z\right)e^{-{\rm i}\left(\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right)z}\textnormal{d}z\ e^{{\rm i}jx}\,.

Fix
\mu_{0}>1
, let
s_{0}>0
associated to
\varrho_{>N}
as per
Definition

2.26
, let
\gamma\leq s-\left(s_{0}+\mu_{0}\right)
.
The term
I_{1}
can be estimated using (
2.34
),
the fact that
\chi_{1}(z\left\langle\xi\right\rangle)
is supported for
\left|z\right|\lesssim 1/\left\langle\xi\right\rangle

and
n>-1
, as

\left|I_{1}\right|\lesssim\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}\int_{\left|z\right|\lesssim 1/\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}\left|\widehat{\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\left(1-\partial_{x}^{2}\right)^{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}}\varrho_{>N}}\left(u;j,z\right)\right|\textnormal{d}z\\
\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n\right)}.

(2.59)

Next we estimate
I_{2}
. After an integration by parts, setting
l=\max\left\{0,\left\lceil 1+n\right\rceil\right\}
, we have from
Eq.

2.58

\displaystyle\left|I_{2}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\left|\tilde{\psi}\left(j,\xi\right)\chi\left(2\xi-j\right)\right|\left|\xi-\frac{j}{2}\right|^{-l}\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3}=l}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|Z_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}\left(z\right)\right|\textnormal{d}z\,.

(2.60)

where

Z_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}\left(z\right)\vcentcolon=\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{1}}\left(\partial_{z}^{l_{1}}\chi_{2}\right)\left(z\left\langle\xi\right\rangle\right)\ \partial_{z}^{l_{2}}\eta\left(z\right)\ \partial_{t}^{k}\widehat{\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l_{3}}\varrho}_{>N}\left(u;j,z\right)\,.

(2.61)

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}

\left|\widehat{\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l_{3}}\varrho}_{>N}\left(u;j,z\right)\right|\lesssim\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l_{3}}\left(1-\partial_{x}^{2}\right)^{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}}\varrho_{>N}\left(u;x,z\right)\right|\lesssim\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left|z\right|^{n-l_{3}}\,.

(2.62)

With computations analogous to the ones performed in
Eqs.

2.54
and
2.55
we obtain using
Eqs.

2.62
and
2.61
, that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|Z_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}\left(z\right)\right|\textnormal{d}z\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\left(l_{1}+l_{3}\right)-\left(n+1\right)},

\displaystyle\text{ if }

\displaystyle l_{1}\neq 0,

(2.63)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|Z_{0,l_{2},l_{3}}\left(z\right)\right|\textnormal{d}z\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left(1+\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{l_{3}-\left(n+1\right)}\right).

Since
l=l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3}>1+n
and
\mu_{0}>1
we obtain, by
Eqs.

2.60
,
2.63
and
2.47
that

\left|I_{2}\right|\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n\right)}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle j\right\rangle^{-\mu_{0}}.

(2.64)

Inserting
Eqs.

2.59
and
2.64
in (
2.58
) we conclude that

\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}a_{>N}\left(u;x,\xi\right)\right|\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n\right)}.

Arguing as in (
2.57
) we thus obtain that, for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a_{>N}\left(u;x,\xi\right)\right|\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{k,s_{0}+\mu_{0}}^{N}\left\|u\right\|_{k,s}\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{-\left(1+n+\beta\right)}

concluding the proof of (
2.50
).

∎

3
The linearized problem at
f=0

The linearized equation (
1.11
)
at
f=0
is

\partial_{t}f=\partial_{x}\,\textnormal{d}\nabla E_{\alpha}(0)f\,.

(3.1)

In this section we prove that the linear Hamiltonian operator

\partial_{x}\,\textnormal{d}\nabla E_{\alpha}(0)

is a Fourier multiplier with symbol

-{\rm i}\,\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)

and we provide its
asymptotic expansion, see Lemmas
3.1
and
3.6
.
We also prove a convexity property of the frequency map

j\mapsto\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
and that
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
are positive
for any
j\geq 2
, whereas
\omega_{\alpha}(0)=\omega_{\alpha}(1)=0
, cf. Remark

3.3
. These latter results do not rely on
oscillatory integrals
expansions and enable to prove
the absence of three-wave resonances in
Lemma

3.5
.

The following result extends
the computations in
[
42
, Section 3]
, valid
for
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)
, to the whole range
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right)
.

Lemma 3.1

(Linearization of
\nabla E_{\alpha}
at zero)
.

For any
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right)
,
it results that

\textnormal{d}\nabla E_{\alpha}(0)=-L_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\,,

(3.2)

where
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)
is the Fourier multiplier operator

L_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\vcentcolon=\ \frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\left[\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)-\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)-\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\right]

(3.3)

with

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\sum_{k=0}^{\left|j\right|-1}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k}\,,

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(0\right)=0\,,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|\xi\right|\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|\xi\right|\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left[\left|\xi\right|^{2}-\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\right]\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),

(3.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \frac{1}{\left|\xi\right|^{2}-\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|\xi\right|\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|\xi\right|\right)}\,.

(3.6)

The map
j\mapsto\mathsf{T}^{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
is a Fourier multiplier in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\max\left(0,\alpha-1\right)}_{0}
and

j\mapsto\mathsf{T}^{\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)

is a Fourier multiplier in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\alpha-1}_{0}
.

By the previous lemma, in Fourier, the linear equation
(
3.1
) amounts to the decoupled
scalar equations

\partial_{t}\hat{f}\left(j\right)+{\rm i}\,\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\hat{f}\left(j\right)=0\,,\qquad j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\,,

(3.7)

with linear frequencies of oscillations

\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\vcentcolon=jL_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
.

Proof of
Lemma

3.1
.

By differentiating

\nabla E_{\alpha}\left(f\right)
in (
1.12
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\textnormal{d}\nabla E_{\alpha}(0)\ \phi=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{2\phi\left(y\right)-\left(\phi\left(x\right)+\phi\left(y\right)\right)\cos\left(x-y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}y

(3.8)

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi^{\prime}\left(y\right)\sin\left(x-y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}y-\frac{\alpha}{4}\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)+\phi\left(y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)-\phi\left(y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}y+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi^{\prime}\left(y\right)\sin\left(x-y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{4}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}y+\frac{c_{\alpha}}{4}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\textnormal{d}y}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \phi\left(x\right)=:\ \sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},\kappa}\phi\,.

We now compute these operators.

Step 1

(Evaluation of
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}
)
.

We claim that

\left(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\phi\right)\left(x\right)\vcentcolon=-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)-\phi\left(y\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos\left(x-y\right)\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}y=-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\phi\,.

(3.9)

Indeed, setting
y=x-z
we have

L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\phi=-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)-\phi\left(x-z\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\alpha/2}}\textnormal{d}z=-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)-\phi\left(x-z\right)}{\left|2\sin\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z\,.

(3.10)

We compute the action of
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}
on

\phi\left(x\right)=\ \sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}=\hat{\phi}(0)+\underbrace{\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}}_{=:\phi_{+}\left(x\right)}+\underbrace{\sum_{j\geq 1}\overline{\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)}e^{-{\rm i}jx}}_{=:\phi_{-}\left(x\right)}
.

By (
3.10
) we immediately get
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\hat{\phi}(0)=0
.
Moreover, by (
3.10
),

L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\phi_{+}\left(x\right)=\ -\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left|4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha/2}}\textnormal{d}z\,.

(3.11)

We compute

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left|4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha/2}}\textnormal{d}z=\ \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left|1-e^{-{\rm i}z}\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z\\
=\ \frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{1-e^{-2{\rm i}jz}}{\left|1-e^{-2{\rm i}z}\right|}\left|1-e^{-2{\rm i}z}\right|^{1-\alpha}\textnormal{d}z=-\frac{2^{1-\alpha}}{{\rm i}\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-{\rm i}z\left(2k+1\right)}\left(\sin z\right)^{1-\alpha}\textnormal{d}z

(3.12)

having also written

\left|1-e^{-2{\rm i}z}\right|=-{\rm i}\left(1-e^{-2{\rm i}z}\right)e^{{\rm i}z}
, for any
z\in\left[0,\pi\right]
.
We use now the identity (cf.
[
64
, p. 8]
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\pi}\sin^{X}\left(z\right)e^{{\rm i}Yz}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{\pi e^{{\rm i}\frac{Y\pi}{2}}\Gamma\left(X+1\right)}{2^{X}\Gamma\left(1+\frac{X+Y}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{X-Y}{2}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\left(X,Y\right)\in\left(-1,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}\,.

(3.13)

Setting
X=1-\alpha
and
Y=-\left(2k+1\right)
,
and using
e^{-{\rm i}\left(2k+1\right)\frac{\pi}{2}}=-{\rm i}\left(-1\right)^{k}
,
we obtain

\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-{\rm i}z\left(2k+1\right)}\left(\sin z\right)^{1-\alpha}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{-{\rm i}\left(-1\right)^{k}\pi\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{2^{1-\alpha}\Gamma\left(1-k-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2+k-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\,.

(3.14)

The following consequence of Euler’s reflection formula (cf.
[
67
]
)

\Gamma\left(z-j\right)=\left(-1\right)^{j-1}\frac{\Gamma\left(-z\right)\Gamma\left(1+z\right)}{\Gamma\left(j+1-z\right)}\,,\qquad z\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Z}\,,\quad j\in\mathbb{Z}\,,

(3.15)

implies, setting
z=1-\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
j=k
, and since

\Gamma\left(1+y\right)=y\ \Gamma\left(y\right)
,

\Gamma\left(1-k-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{k-1}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}-1\right)\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{k}\frac{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}\,.

(3.16)

By (
3.14
)-(
3.16
) we deduce

\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-{\rm i}z\left(2k+1\right)}\left(\sin z\right)^{1-\alpha}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{-{\rm i}\pi}{2^{1-\alpha}}\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}\,.

(3.17)

Consequently, by (
3.12
) and (
3.17
), we conclude that for any
j\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left|4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha/2}}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k}=\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(j\right)

defined in (
3.4
),
which in turn, recalling (
3.11
), implies that

L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\phi_{+}\left(x\right)=-\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(j\right)\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)e^{{\rm i}jx}
.
Since
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}
is a real operator

\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(-j\right)=\overline{\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(j\right)}=\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(j\right)

which, combined with
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},1}\hat{\phi}(0)=0
, gives us (
3.9
).

Step 2

(Evaluation of
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},2}
)
.

We claim that

\left(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},2}\,\phi\right)\left(x\right)=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \left|D\right|^{2}\ \left(\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\phi\right)(x)\text{ .}

(3.18)

Setting
y=x-z
we have

(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},2}\,\phi)(x)=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\sin z}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}z
.
As

\frac{\sin z}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=\frac{1}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\partial_{z}\left(\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
, integrating by parts

(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},2}\,\phi)(x)=\ -\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\left[2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\right]^{2}}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\partial_{z}\big[\phi^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\big]}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{\left[2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\right]^{2}}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}\left(x-z\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\textnormal{d}z\,.

(3.19)

For
j\geq 0
we compute

\displaystyle I_{j}\vcentcolon=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-{\rm i}jz}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\frac{2^{2-\alpha}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{-{\rm i}\ 2jz}\left(\sin z\right)^{2-\alpha}\textnormal{d}z

and applying
Eq.

3.13
with

X=2-\alpha
,
Y=-2j
, and using
\Gamma(x+1)=x\Gamma(x)
, we obtain

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\left(-1\right)^{j}\frac{(2-\alpha)\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-j-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2+j-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\,.

We use now the identities

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)=\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\ \Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}-j\right)=\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}-j\right)\left(-1\right)^{j}\frac{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\,,

which follows by
\Gamma\left(1+z\right)=z\ \Gamma\left(z\right)
and (
3.15
)
(with
z=-\alpha/2
and
j\leadsto j-1
),
to deduce, for any
j\geq 0
,

I_{j}=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{2-\alpha}{\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}-j^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}=-2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(j\right)

with
\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}
defined in (
3.6
).
Since
I_{j}=I_{-j}
we conclude that

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=-2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\,.

(3.20)

By (
3.19
) and
(
3.20
) we deduce (
3.18
).

Step 3

(Evaluation of
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},3}
)
.

The action of the operator
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},3}
in (
3.8
)
on a function
\phi(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)\ e^{{\rm i}jx}
is,
setting
y=x-z
and using (
3.20
),

\left(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},3}\phi\right)(x)=\ \frac{c_{\alpha}}{4}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{\phi}\left(j\right)\ e^{{\rm i}jx}\ \mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\ -\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\ \left(\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\phi\right)(x)\,.

(3.21)

Step 4

(Evaluation of
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},4}
)
.

By (
3.20
) with
j=0
and (
3.6
) it results
that
L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},4}
in (
3.8
)
is

\left(L_{\nabla E_{\alpha},4}\phi\right)(x)=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{4}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi\left(x\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\ \phi\left(x\right)\,.

(3.22)

In conclusion, by (
3.8
), (
3.9
), (
3.18
), (
3.21
), (
3.22
) we deduce
that
\textnormal{d}\nabla E_{\alpha}(0)

is equal to
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)
in (
3.3
).

Step 5

(
\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\max\left(0,\alpha-1\right)}

and
\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\alpha-1}
)
.

We start with
\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}(|\xi|)
in (
3.5
)
which is defined on
\mathbb{R}
. We recall
the asymptotic expansion for
\left|\xi\right|\to\infty
, see

[
67
, Eq. (5.11.13)]
,

\frac{\Gamma\left(\xi+a\right)}{\Gamma\left(\xi+b\right)}-\xi^{a-b}\left(\sum_{\kappa=0}^{N}\frac{G_{\kappa}\left(a,b\right)}{\xi^{\kappa}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\xi\right|^{\left(a-b\right)-\left(1+N\right)}\right)\,\\
\begin{aligned} G_{0}\left(a,b\right)=1\,,\ G_{1}\left(a,b\right)=\frac{\left(a-b\right)\left(a+b-1\right)}{2}\,,&&\forall N\in\mathbb{N}\,,\ \left|\textnormal{Arg}\ \xi\right|<\pi\,,\end{aligned}

(3.23)

which involves holomorphic functions. We can focus on the case
\text{Re}(\xi)>0
. We claim that formula
(
3.23
) implies automatically the estimates for the derivatives

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\xi+a\right)}{\Gamma\left(\xi+b\right)}\right)\right|\lesssim_{\mu}\xi^{a-b-\mu}\quad\text{ for large $\xi>0$ and for any $\mu\in\mathbb{N}$}\,.

(3.24)

Case
\mu=0
of (
3.24
) follows trivially from (
3.23
).
For any
\mu\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
and for
N_{1}\gg\mu\geq 1
, let us set

\displaystyle M_{1}\left(\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\xi^{a-b}\sum_{\kappa=0}^{N}\frac{G_{\kappa}\left(a,b\right)}{\xi^{\kappa}},

\displaystyle M_{2}\left(\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\xi^{a-b}\sum_{\kappa=N+1}^{N+N_{1}}\frac{G_{\kappa}\left(a,b\right)}{\xi^{\kappa}},

\displaystyle E\left(\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{\Gamma\left(\xi+a\right)}{\Gamma\left(\xi+b\right)}-\left(M_{1}\left(\xi\right)+M_{2}\left(\xi\right)\right)\,.

Obviously
M_{1}\left(\xi\right)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{a-b}_{0}
and
M_{2}\left(\xi\right)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{a-b-\left(N+1\right)}_{0}
. For
\xi\gg 1
,
E
is holomorphic in
B\left(\xi,2\right)
. Thus

\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}E\left(\xi\right)=\frac{c_{\mu}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\partial B\left(\xi,1\right)}\frac{E\left(\zeta\right)}{\left(\zeta-\xi\right)^{1+\mu}}\ \textnormal{d}\zeta

by the Cauchy formula.
Moreover (
3.23
) is true in
B\left(\xi,2\right)
and so, by
\left|\zeta\right|\sim\left|\xi\right|
,

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}E\left(\xi\right)\right|\lesssim_{\mu}\int_{\partial B\left(\xi,1\right)}\frac{\left|\zeta\right|^{a-b-\left(1+N+N_{1}\right)}}{\left|\zeta-\xi\right|^{1+\mu}}\ \left|\textnormal{d}\zeta\right|\lesssim_{\mu}\left|\xi\right|^{a-b-\left(1+N+N_{1}\right)}\lesssim_{\mu}\left|\xi\right|^{a-b-\left(1+N+\mu\right)},

which implies

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\xi+a\right)}{\Gamma\left(\xi+b\right)}-M_{1}\left(\xi\right)\right)\right|\leq\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}M_{2}\left(\xi\right)\right|+\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\mu}E\left(\xi\right)\right|\lesssim_{\mu}\left|\xi\right|^{a-b-\left(1+N+\mu\right)}\,.

This proves (
3.24
).
From (
3.24
) we conclude that

\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
in (
3.5
) is a Fourier multiplier of order
\alpha-1
.

We now consider
\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
defined in (
3.4
).
For any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
the discrete derivative of
\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
is

(\Delta\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha})\left(j\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(j+1\right)-\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(j\right)=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j}=\frac{\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\left(j\right)}{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j}\,.

Since
\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}
is a symbol of order
\alpha-1
we deduce that

\left|\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\right|\lesssim 1+j^{\alpha-1}
and,
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
the discrete derivatives satisfy

\left|\left(\Delta^{\ell}\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\right)\left(j\right)\right|\lesssim j^{\alpha-1-\ell}
.
By
[
72
, Lemma 7.1.1]
there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
extension of
\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}
to the whole
\mathbb{R}
which is a symbol of order
\max\left(\alpha-1,0\right)
.

The proof of
Lemma

3.1
is complete.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

For
\alpha\neq 1
the Fourier multiplier

\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
in (
3.4
) is equal to

\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+\left|j\right|)}{\Gamma(1+\left|j\right|-\tfrac{\alpha}{2})}-\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2})}\right)

as follows by induction.

Remark 3.3
.

The first linear frequency

\omega_{\alpha}(1)=0
. This is equivalent to prove that
L_{\alpha}(1)=0
, that,
in view of (
3.3
)-(
3.5
), amounts to show that

\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(1\right)-\mathsf{T}^{2}_{\alpha}\left(1\right)-\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\\
=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\left[\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right]=0\,.

This holds true because, using the identity
\Gamma(y+1)=y\ \Gamma(y)
,

\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\frac{\alpha}{2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}-\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\\
=\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\left[1-\frac{\alpha}{2}-\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\right]=0\,.

The fact that the first frequency
\omega_{\alpha}(1)=0

is zero has a dynamical proof. Indeed,
in view of the translation invariance of the problem,
the patch equation (
1.11
) possesses the vector prime integral

\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\sqrt{1+2f\left(x\right)}-1\right)\vec{\gamma}\left(x\right)\ \textnormal{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{T}}f\left(x\right)(\cos x,\sin x)\ \textnormal{d}x+O\left(\left\|f\right\|^{2}\right)\,.

(3.25)

Let us consider a dynamical system

\dot{f}=Y\left(f\right)
with
Y(0)=0
and
A\vcentcolon=\textnormal{d}Y(0)
.
If
b\left(f\right)
is a prime integral then

\nabla b\left(f\right)\cdot Y\left(f\right)=0
,
\forall f
.
Hence, differentiating and since
Y(0)=0
, we obtain

\nabla b(0)\cdot Af=0
,
\forall f
.
If
A
is non singular then
\nabla b(0)=0
, i.e. the prime integral

b
is quadratic at
f=0
. Here the linear operator
A
(cf. (
3.7
))
is degenerate in the one-Fourier mode on which (
3.25
) has a linear component in
f
.

The other linear frequencies
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
,
j\neq 0,\pm 1
, are all
different from zero.

Lemma 3.4

(Convexity of
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
)
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right)
.
The frequency map

j\mapsto\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)=j\,L_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
,

j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
where
L_{\alpha}
is computed in
Lemma

3.1
, is odd and
satisfies the convexity property

\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\vcentcolon=\omega_{\alpha}\left(j+1\right)+\omega_{\alpha}\left(j-1\right)-2\ \omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{2^{1-\alpha}\Gamma^{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}-1+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\alpha j>0\,,\quad\forall\ j\geq 1\,.

(3.26)

The linear frequencies
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
are different from zero
for any
\left|j\right|\geq 2
,
in particular
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)>0
and increasing for any
j\geq 2
.

Proof.

In view of
Lemma

3.1
, for any
j\geq 1
,
and the identity
\Gamma(1+y)=y\Gamma(y)
,
the second discrete derivative
\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
is equal to

\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\left\{\left(j+1\right)\sum_{k=0}^{j}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}+\left(j-1\right)\sum_{k=0}^{j-2}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}-2j\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+k\right)}\right.\\
-\left.\left(j+1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j+1\right)}-\left(j-1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}+2j\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\right\}\,.

(3.27)

The first term inside the above bracket is equal to

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\left(j+1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}-\left(j-1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}

(3.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\left[\left(j+1\right)\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)-\left(j-1\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\right]=\ \frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\ \alpha j\,.

Writing the terms in the 2nd line of the bracket in (
3.27
) as

\displaystyle-\left(j+1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j+1\right)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\ \left(j+1\right)\left(\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\left(\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right),

(3.29)

\displaystyle-\left(j-1\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\ \left(j-1\right)\left(-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\left(1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right),

\displaystyle 2j\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+j-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\ 2j\left(j^{2}-\left(1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\right)\,,

we conclude by
Eqs.

3.27
,
3.28
and
3.29
and
since
c_{\alpha}=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{2^{1-\alpha}\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}
(cf. (
1.5
)), that

\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)=\frac{1}{2^{1-\alpha}\left(2-\alpha\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma^{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}-1+j\right)}{\Gamma\left(2-\frac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)}\ X_{\alpha}\left(j\right)

where

X_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\vcentcolon=\alpha j-\left(j+1\right)\left(\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\left(\tfrac{\alpha}{2}-1+j\right)-\left(j-1\right)\left(-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)\left(1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}+j\right)+2j\left(j^{2}-\left(1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\right)=(2-\alpha)\alpha j\,.

This proves (
3.26
).
The positivity of
\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
in (
3.26
) follows because
the function
\Gamma
is positive on positive numbers.
Finally, the convexity property (
3.26
)
and
\omega_{\alpha}\left(0\right)=\omega_{\alpha}\left(1\right)=0
(cf. Remark
3.3
) imply that

\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)>0
and increasing for any
j\geq 2
.
∎

The next lemma
is crucial for the normal form construction of Section
5
.

Lemma 3.5

(Absence of three wave interactions)
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right)
.
For any
n,j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
satisfying

k=j+n
, it results

\left|\omega_{\alpha}\left(k\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(n\right)\right|\geq\omega_{\alpha}\left(2\right)>0\,.

(3.30)

Proof.

Since
the map
j\mapsto\omega_{\alpha}(j)
is odd
and strictly increasing for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
it is sufficient to consider the case
k\geq j\geq n\geq 1
,
k=j+n
.
Then, using that

\omega_{\alpha}(0)=\omega_{\alpha}\left(1\right)=0
,
defining
A_{\alpha}(\ell)\vcentcolon=\omega_{\alpha}(\ell)-\omega_{\alpha}(\ell-1)
,
we write by a telescoping expansion,

\displaystyle\omega_{\alpha}\left(k\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(n\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{j+n}\left(\omega_{\alpha}\left(q\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(q-1\right)\right)-\sum_{q=1}^{j}\left(\omega_{\alpha}\left(q\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(q-1\right)\right)-\sum_{q=1}^{n}\left(\omega_{\alpha}\left(q\right)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(q-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{n}\left(A_{\alpha}\left(q+j\right)-A_{\alpha}\left(q\right)\right)=\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{j}\left(A_{\alpha}\left(q+q^{\prime}\right)-A_{\alpha}\left(q+q^{\prime}-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{j}\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(q+q^{\prime}-1\right)\geq\triangle^{2}\omega_{\alpha}\left(1\right)=\omega_{\alpha}\left(2\right)>0

by (
3.26
) and

Lemma

3.4
.
This proves (
3.30
).
∎

We finally prove an asymptotic expansion of
the frequencies
\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
.
We use the notation
\sum_{j=p_{1}}^{p_{2}}a_{j}\equiv 0
if
p_{2}<p_{1}
.
We denote
m_{\beta}

a real Fourier multiplier of order
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, and
c_{\alpha}^{\kappa}
real constants, which may vary from line to line.

Lemma 3.6

(Asymptotic behavior of
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
)
.

Let

\mathbb{V}_{\alpha}\vcentcolon=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\alpha c_{\alpha}}{2-\alpha}\frac{\Gamma\left(1-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}&\alpha&\neq 1\,,\\
&\frac{1}{\pi}\left\{\left(\gamma_{\textnormal{EM}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}-2\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}+k}-\frac{1}{k}\left(1-\frac{1}{2k}\right)\right]\right\}&\alpha&=1\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(3.31)

where

\gamma_{\textnormal{EM}}\vcentcolon=\left(\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{k}\right)-\log n
is the Euler-Mascheroni constant.

Then the symbol
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)
in
Lemma

3.1
has the following
asymptotic expansion: for any
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbb{N},\ \mathcal{K}\geq 3
,

•

If
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)\cup\left(1,2\right)
there exists real constants
c^{\kappa}_{\alpha},\ \kappa\in\left\{3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}-1\right\}
and
a Fourier multiplier
m_{\alpha-\mathcal{K}}
of order
\alpha-\mathcal{K}
such that

L_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\mathbb{V}_{\alpha}+\underbrace{\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(3-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\alpha-1}}_{\vcentcolon=c^{1}_{\alpha}}\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-1}+\sum_{\kappa=3}^{\mathcal{K}-1}c_{\alpha}^{\kappa}\left|j\right|^{\alpha-\kappa}+m_{\alpha-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\,.

(3.32)

•

If
\alpha=1
there exists real constants
c^{\kappa}_{1},\ \kappa\in\left\{3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}-1\right\}
and a Fourier multiplier
m_{1-\mathcal{K}}
of order
1-\mathcal{K}
such that

L_{1}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\mathbb{V}_{1}+\frac{1}{\pi}\log\left|j\right|+\sum_{\kappa=3}^{\mathcal{K}-1}c_{1}^{\kappa}\left|j\right|^{1-\kappa}+m_{1-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\,.

Note that in the expansion (
3.32
) there is not a term as

c_{\alpha}^{2}|j|^{\alpha-2}
and that
\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-1}
is,
for
\alpha\in(1,2)
, positive and tends to infinity,
whereas, for
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)
, it is negative and tends to zero.

We provide for completeness the expansion also in the cases
\alpha=1
and

\alpha\in(0,1)
, although not needed for the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma

3.6
is a direct consequence of
(
3.5
), (
3.3
) and (
1.5
)
and the following lemma.

Lemma 3.7
.

For any
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbb{N},\mathcal{K}\geq 3
, the following holds:

•

if
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)\cup\left(1,2\right)
, there exist real constants
c_{\alpha}^{\kappa}
,
\ \kappa\in\left\{3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}-1\right\}
such that

\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\frac{\Gamma\left(1-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-1}+\sum_{\kappa=3}^{\mathcal{K}-1}c_{\alpha}^{\kappa}\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-\kappa}+m_{\alpha-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\,.

(3.33)

•

if
\alpha=1
there exist real constants
c_{1}^{\kappa},\ \kappa\in\left\{3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}-1\right\}
such that

\mathsf{T}^{1}_{1}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\frac{1}{\pi}\left\{\log\left|j\right|+\left(\gamma_{\textnormal{EM}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}+k}-\frac{1}{k}\left(1-\frac{1}{2k}\right)\right]\right\}+\sum_{\kappa=3}^{\mathcal{K}-1}c_{1}^{\kappa}\ \left|j\right|^{-\kappa}+m_{1-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right);

(3.34)

•

if
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right)
there exist real constants
c_{\alpha}^{\kappa},\ \kappa\in\left\{3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}-1\right\}
such that

\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|j\right|\right)=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{1}{\left|j\right|^{2}-\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\ \left[\left|j\right|^{\alpha-1}+\sum_{\kappa=3}^{\mathcal{K}-1}c_{\alpha}^{\kappa}\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-\kappa}+m_{\alpha-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\right].

(3.35)

Proof.

By the proof of
Lemma

3.1
(below (
3.23
)) we know that

\frac{\Gamma\left(\xi+a\right)}{\Gamma\left(\xi+b\right)}-\xi^{a-b}\sum_{\kappa=0}^{N}\frac{G_{\kappa}\left(a,b\right)}{\xi^{\kappa}}=m_{a-b-\left(1+N\right)}\left(\xi\right)\,,

where
m_{a-b-\left(1+N\right)}\left(\xi\right)
is a Fourier multiplier in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{a-b-\left(1+N\right)}_{0}
,
and therefore, for any
\mathcal{K}\geq 3
,

\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|j\right|\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\left|j\right|\right)}=\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-1}+\sum_{\kappa=2}^{\mathcal{K}-1}G_{\kappa}\left(\frac{\alpha}{2},1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\ \left|j\right|^{\alpha-\left(1+\kappa\right)}+m_{\alpha-\mathcal{K}}\left(\left|j\right|\right)\,,

(3.36)

where we exploited that
G_{0}(a,b)=1
and

G_{1}\left(\frac{\alpha}{2},1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)=0
, by (
3.23
).
By Remark
3.2
and (
3.36
) we deduce (
3.33
) for
\mathcal{K}=3
.
Finally (
3.34
) for

\alpha=1
follows by the asymptotic estimate
of the harmonic numbers

\sum_{k=1}^{j}k^{-1}=\gamma_{\textnormal{EM}}+\log\left(j\right)+\frac{1}{2j}+m_{-2}\left(j\right)
.
∎

4
Paralinearization of the Hamiltonian scalar field

The main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 4.1

(Paralinearization of the
\alpha
-SQG patch equation)
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(0,1\right)\cup\left(1,2\right)
.
Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho\geq 0
.
For any
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
there exist
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that,
if
f\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
solves
Eq.

1.11
then

\partial_{t}f+\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\nu\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]\ f\\
=R\left(f\right)f

(4.1)

where

•

L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is the
real valued Fourier multiplier of order
\max\{0,\alpha-1\}

defined in
Lemma

3.1
;

•

\nu\left(f;x\right),V\left(f;x\right)
are real valued functions
in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(see
Definition

2.7
);

•

P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(see
Definition

2.2
) satisfying (
2.20
);

•

R\left(f\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(see
Definition

2.17
).

Note that, since the symbol

\left(1+\nu\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+V\left(f;x\right)

is real,
the vector field in (
4.1
)
is linearly Hamiltonian up to zero order operators.

4.1
Isolating the integral terms

Notation.

In this section we use the following auxiliary functions

\displaystyle r=r\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\sqrt{1+2f\left(x\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{z}f\vcentcolon=f\left(x\right)-f\left(x-z\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{z}f\vcentcolon=\frac{\delta_{z}f}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\,,\ \forall z\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\{0\}\,.

(4.2)

We shall denote by
P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

(see
Definition

2.2
)
by
R\left(f\right)
a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

(see
Definition

2.17
)
and by
R\left(f;z\right)

a Kernel-smoothing operator in

\Sigma K\mathcal{R}^{-\rho,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(see
Definition

2.33
), whose explicit expression may vary from line to line.

Note that
r\left(f;x\right)
is a function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

and, according to
Definition

2.31
,

\delta_{z}\in\widetilde{K}\mathcal{M}^{\ 1,1}_{0}\,.

(4.3)

In view of (
1.12
)
and performing the change of variable
y=x-z
,
the gradient
\nabla E_{\alpha}\left(f\right)
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\nabla E_{\alpha}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(f\right)+\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(2\right)}\left(f\right)\,,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{1+2f\left(x-z\right)-\sqrt{1+2f\left(x\right)}\ \sqrt{1+2f\left(x-z\right)}\cos z}{\left[2\left(1+f\left(x\right)+f\left(x-z\right)-\sqrt{1+2f\left(x\right)}\sqrt{1+2f\left(x-z\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(2\right)}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\sqrt{\frac{1+2f\left(x\right)}{1+2f\left(x-z\right)}}\ f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\sin z}{\left[2\left(1+f\left(x\right)+f\left(x-z\right)-\sqrt{1+2f\left(x\right)}\sqrt{1+2f\left(x-z\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,.

Then, recalling the notation in (
4.2
), we write

\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(f\right)=\ \frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{r^{2}-2\delta_{z}f-r\ \sqrt{r^{2}-2\delta_{z}f}\cos z}{\left[2\left(r^{2}-\delta_{z}f-r\sqrt{r^{2}-2\delta_{z}f}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\ \frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ r^{2-\alpha}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\ \textnormal{d}z

(4.5)

with

\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\vcentcolon=\ \frac{1-2\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\,,

(4.6)

and

\nabla E^{\left(2\right)}_{\alpha}\left(f\right)=\ \frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\underbrace{\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\ f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\sin z\ \textnormal{d}z}_{=:\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)}=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\ \mathcal{J}\left(f\right)

(4.7)

with

\mathsf{G}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}}}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}.

(4.8)

By (
4.4
), recalling (
2.3
) and that

\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(0\right)
is a constant, using
(
4.5
),
(
4.7
), the equation (
1.11
)
can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f

\displaystyle=\ \partial_{x}\left[\left(\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(f\right)-\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(1\right)}\left(0\right)\right)+\nabla E_{\alpha}^{\left(2\right)}\left(f\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \partial_{x}\left[\left(r^{2-\alpha}\Delta I\left(f\right)\right)+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)+\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\ \mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\right]

(4.9)

where

\Delta I\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\,.

(4.10)

By (
3.22
), (
4.6
) we get

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\textnormal{d}z=\frac{1}{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\,.

(4.11)

The terms
\Delta I\left(f\right)
and
\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)
are yet not in a suitable form to be paralinearized, since the nonlinear convolution kernels need to be desingularized
at
z=0
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The term
\Delta I\left(f\right)
in (
4.10
) can be written as

\Delta I\left(f\right)=\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+R\left(f\right)f

(4.12)

where
R\left(f\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, and

\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]{\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}}\textnormal{d}z

(4.13)

with

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left(\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}\left(2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}=\left[-\frac{2-\frac{\cos z}{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}}{\left[2\left(1-2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right.\\
\ \ \left.+\alpha\frac{\left(1-\frac{\cos z}{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}\right)\left(1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)}{\left[2\left(1-2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+1}}\right]\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}\,.\end{multlined}

The term
\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)
in (
4.7
)
can be written as

\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\ f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z

(4.15)

where

\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{G}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)\ \left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}{\left[2\left(1-2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\,.

(4.16)

The functions
z\mapsto\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)
,
\mathsf{j}=1,2,

are
{2\pi}
-periodic.

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

2.25
to (
4.10
) we get

\displaystyle\Delta I\left(f\right)=\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\textnormal{d}z+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits R\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\textnormal{d}z

(4.17)

where
R
is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and, recalling (
4.6
),

\left(\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)=-\frac{2-\frac{\cos z}{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}}}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}+\alpha\frac{\left(1-\frac{\cos z}{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}}\right)\left(1-2\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+1}}.

(4.18)

In view of (
4.18
), (
4.14
)
we have that

\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)=\left(\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}\left(2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}

so that the first term on the right hand side of (
4.17
) is equal to
\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)
in (
4.13
).
Notice that, since
\Delta_{z+2\pi}f=-\Delta_{z}f
and
\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z+2\pi}\left(-\mathsf{X}\right)=\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)
(cf. (
4.14
)),
the map
z\mapsto\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)
is
2\pi
-periodic.
Similarly
z\mapsto\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)
is
2\pi
-periodic.

We now prove that

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits R\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)\textnormal{d}z=R\left(f\right)f\qquad\text{where}\qquad R\left(f\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,.

(4.19)

We write

R\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)=R\left(M\left(f;z\right)f\right)M\left(f;z\right)f

where

M\left(f;z\right)=M_{1}\left(f\right)+M_{2}\left(f;z\right)\,,\quad M_{1}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{1+2f}\,,\quad M_{2}\left(f;z\right)=-\frac{\mathsf{t}_{-z}}{1+2f}\,.

Remark
2.16

shows that
M_{1}\left(f\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{0,0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
2.34
,
Item

2
proves that
M_{2}\left(f;z\right)
belongs to
\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{0,0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. Thus

M\left(f;z\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{0,0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and

2.34
,
Items

4
and
2
give that

R\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)=R\left(M\left(f;z\right)f\right)M\left(f;z\right)f=R\left(f;z\right)f

for some Kernel-smoothing operator

R\left(f;z\right)
in
\Sigma K\mathcal{R}^{-\rho,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. Finally
Lemma

2.35
implies (
4.19
).
∎

Plugging (
4.12
) in (
4.9
) we obtain

\partial_{t}f=\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\ \partial_{x}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\left(\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+R\left(f\right)f\right)+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)+\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\ \mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\right].

(4.20)

4.2
Analysis of the nonlinear convolution kernels

The goal of this section is to
represent the nonlinear convolution kernels in (
4.13
)
and
(
4.15
) as Kernel-functions
according to
Definition

2.26
. In
Section

4.4

we shall consider
as well the convolution kernel

\mathsf{K}^{3}_{\alpha,z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\vcentcolon=\left(\mathsf{G}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}\left(\mathsf{X}\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)\ \sin z\left|2\sin(z/2)\right|^{\alpha}=\left[\frac{\frac{1}{\left(1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{3/2}}}{\left[2\left(1-2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\right.\\
\left.+\alpha\frac{\left(1-\frac{\cos z}{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}}{\left[2\left(1-2\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+1}}\right]\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}\ \sin z\,.

(4.21)

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}(\mathsf{X})
,
\alpha\in\left(0,2\right),\ \mathsf{j}=1,2,3,
be the functions
defined in (
4.14
), (
4.16
) and (
4.21
). Then

\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{F}^{0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

(4.22)

is a Kernel function, which admits the expansion

\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\ \sin z+\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},2}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}+\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\,,

(4.23)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,\underline{p}\left(\mathsf{j},l\right)}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,

\displaystyle\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{F}^{3}_{K,0,\underline{q}\left(\mathsf{j}\right)}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,

\displaystyle\underline{q}\left(\mathsf{j}\right)\vcentcolon=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1&&\text{ if }\ \mathsf{j}=1,2\,,\\
&0&&\text{ if }\ \mathsf{j}=3\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.24)

with
\underline{p}\left(\mathsf{j},l\right)\in\left\{0,1\right\}

and constant
functions

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{2,0}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{3,0}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)\\
\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{2,1}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{3,1}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)\\
\mathsf{K}^{1,2}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{2,2}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)&\mathsf{K}^{3,2}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-1&1&0\\
0&0&1+\frac{\alpha}{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)&0&0\\
\end{array}\right).

Proof.

The statement (
4.22
)
follows by (
4.23
)-(
4.24
) that we now prove.
We first claim that for any
R>0
, there exists
\varepsilon_{R}>0
such that the functions

\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\mathsf{y}}{1+2\mathsf{x}}\right)\,,\qquad w\vcentcolon=2\sin\left(z/2\right)\,,

(4.31)

where
\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\cdot\right)
,
\mathsf{j}=1,2,3
are defined
in
(
4.14
), (
4.16
) and (
4.21
),
are
analytic in
(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},w)
in the domain

|\mathsf{x}|\leq\varepsilon_{R}\,,\qquad|\mathsf{y}|\leq\varepsilon_{R}\,,\qquad|w|\leq R\,,

(4.32)

and there exists
C_{R}>0
such that

\left|\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)\right|\leq C_{R}
in this domain.
Let us prove the analiticity of

\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)
.
Substituting
\mathsf{X}=\frac{\mathsf{y}}{1+2\mathsf{x}}
,
w=2\sin\left(z/2\right)
and

\cos(z)=1-\frac{w^{2}}{2}
in (
4.14
) we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{1}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2-\frac{1-\frac{w^{2}}{2}}{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}}}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}w-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}+\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}\frac{w^{2}}{2}\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\left|w\right|^{\alpha}

(4.33a)

\displaystyle\ \ +\alpha\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}}\frac{\left(\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}-1+\frac{w^{2}}{2}\right)\left(1-2\mathsf{X}w-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}+\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}\frac{w^{2}}{2}\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\mathsf{X}w-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}+\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}\frac{w^{2}}{2}\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2}}}\left|w\right|^{\alpha}\,.

(4.33b)

Since the function

1-\mathsf{X}w-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}=\left(\mathsf{X}w\right)^{2}+O\left(\mathsf{X}w\right)^{3}
is analytic in
\mathsf{X}w
small,
the function in (
4.33a
) is analytic in the domain
(
4.32
) for
\varepsilon_{R}
small enough.
Furthermore, noting that
the functions

\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}-1=-\mathsf{X}w+O(\mathsf{X}w)^{2}
and

1-2\mathsf{X}w-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}w}=-\mathsf{X}w+O(\mathsf{X}w)^{2}
are analytic in
\mathsf{X}w
small,
we deduce that also (
4.33b
) is analytic
in
(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},w)
in the domain
(
4.32
).
The analiticity of

\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{2}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)

and
\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{3}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)

follow similarly.

Then by Cauchy integral formula,

\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)=\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}=0}^{\infty}\underbrace{\frac{1}{p_{1}!p_{2}!}\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(0,0\right)}_{=:\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)}{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\quad\text{where}\quad\left|\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(0,0\right)\right|\leq p_{1}!p_{2}!C_{R}\varepsilon_{R}^{-p_{1}-p_{2}}\,.

(4.34)

In view of (
4.31
) we have

\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(\mathsf{x},0\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)
for any
\mathsf{x}
, and therefore

\mathsf{k}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha;0,0}\left(z\right)=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\,,\qquad\mathsf{k}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha;0,p_{2}}\left(z\right)\equiv 0\,,\ \forall p_{2}\geq 1\,,

(4.35)

and, by (
4.14
), (
4.16
), (
4.21
), one computes that

\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\left(2\sin\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)\right)^{2}-1\,,\qquad\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)=1\,,\qquad\mathsf{K}^{3}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)=\left(1+\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\sin\left(z\right)\,.

(4.36)

Choosing above
R=4
and
\underline{{\varepsilon}}>0
such that

\left|\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|\leq 2
for
\left|\textnormal{Im}z\right|\leq\underline{{\varepsilon}}
, we
deduce that
each
\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{p_{1}!p_{2}!}\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(0,0\right)
,
\mathsf{j}=1,2,3
,
satisfies

\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)\right|\leq l!\underline{{\varepsilon}}^{-l}C_{R}\varepsilon_{R}^{-p_{1}-p_{2}}\,,\qquad\forall z\in\mathbb{R}\,,\ l\geq 0\,,p_{1},p_{2}\geq 0\,.

(4.37)

Now, in view of (
4.31
), (
4.34
) and (
4.35
) we expand

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{1+2f}\right)={\mathsf{J}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,w}\left(f,\Delta_{z}f\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)+\sum_{p\geq 1}\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+p_{2}=p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)\ f^{p_{2}}\left(\Delta_{z}f\right)^{p_{1}}}_{{=:\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)}}

(4.38)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)+\sum_{p=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)+\underbrace{\sum_{p>N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)}_{=:\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)}\,.

We claim that for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell=0,\ldots,7
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{\ell}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\

\displaystyle\in\widetilde{K}\mathcal{F}^{0}_{p}

(4.39)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{\ell}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)

\displaystyle\in K\mathcal{F}^{0}_{K,0,N+1}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]\,.

(4.40)

Step 1

(Proof of (
4.39
))
.

We expand in Fourier

\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)=\sum_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)^{p}}\partial_{z}^{l}\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)\ f_{j_{1}}\cdots f_{j_{p}}e^{{\rm i}\left(j_{1}+\cdots+j_{p}\right)x}

(4.41)

where, in view of (
4.38
) and (
4.2
),

\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)\vcentcolon=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+p_{2}=p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)\prod_{q=1}^{p_{1}}\triangle_{j_{q}}\left(z\right)\,,\qquad\quad\triangle_{j_{q}}\left(z\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}j_{q}z}}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\,.

For any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\partial_{z}^{l}\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{2}+l_{1,1}+\cdots+l_{1,p_{1}}=l\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+p_{2}=p\end{subarray}}\ \partial_{z}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{\alpha;p_{1},p_{2}}^{\mathsf{j}}\left(z\right)\prod_{q=1}^{p_{1}}\partial_{z}^{l_{1,q}}\triangle_{j_{q}}\left(z\right).

(4.42)

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
, we may write

\triangle_{j}\left(z\right)=\frac{1-e^{-{\rm i}jz}}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}={\rm i}\,\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{\left|j\right|-1}e^{-{\rm i}\textnormal{sgn}\left(j\right)\left(j^{\prime}+\textnormal{sgn}\left(j\right)\frac{1}{2}\right)z}

and then, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\triangle_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\left|j\right|}\left(j^{\prime}\right)^{l}\lesssim_{l}\left|j\right|^{l+1}\,,\quad\forall z\in\mathbb{R}\,.

(4.43)

By (
4.37
), (
4.43
),
we estimate (
4.42
) as (the constant
\varepsilon_{4}

is the one in (
4.37
) for
R=4
)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{2}+l_{1,1}+\cdots+l_{1,p_{1}}=l\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+p_{2}=p\end{subarray}}C_{l}\ \varepsilon_{4}^{-p_{1}-p_{2}}\prod_{q=1}^{p_{1}}\left|j_{q}\right|^{l_{1,q}+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{l}\ p^{2}\left(\frac{l}{\varepsilon_{4}}\right)^{p}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{l}\prod_{q=1}^{p_{1}}\left|j_{q}\right|\ \leq C_{l}^{p}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{l}\ \prod_{q=1}^{p}\left|j_{q}\right|

(4.44)

for some constant
C_{l}>0
, for any
z\in\mathbb{T}
.
The bound (
4.44
)
implies, recalling
Definition

2.26
,
the claim (
4.39
).

Step 2

(Proof of (
4.40
))
.

Recalling (
4.38
) and
(
4.41
), we have,
for any
0\leq k\leq K
,
l,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\sum_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)^{p}}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{\gamma}\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)\right|\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\left(f_{j_{1}}\cdots f_{j_{p}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\sum_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)^{p}}\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{p}=k}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{\gamma}\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\hat{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}\left(z\right)\right|\prod_{q=1}^{p}\left|\partial_{t}^{k_{q}}f_{j_{q}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\sum_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)^{p}}\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{p}=k}C_{l}^{p}\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{\gamma+l}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\left|j_{q}\right|\left|\partial_{t}^{k_{q}}f_{j_{q}}\right|

using (
4.44
).
Then, assuming with no loss of generality
that
\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|=\max\{\left|j_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|j_{p}\right|\}=\left|j_{1}\right|
we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\sum_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}\in\left(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)^{p}}\sum_{k_{1}+\ldots+k_{p}=k}C_{l}^{p}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\left|j_{q}\right|^{-2}\left\|\partial_{t}^{k_{q}}f\right\|_{3}\right)\ \left|j_{1}\right|^{-2}\left\|\partial_{t}^{k_{1}}f\right\|_{3+\gamma+l},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\ C_{l}^{p}\ \sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{p}=0}^{k}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\left\|\partial_{t}^{k_{q}}f\right\|_{3}\right)\left\|\partial_{t}^{k_{1}}f\right\|_{3+\gamma+l}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{p>N}\left(C_{l}k\right)^{p}\ \left\|f\right\|_{k,3+\alpha k}^{p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{k,3+\gamma+l+\alpha k}

recalling (
2.5
).
Summing in
p
and
setting
s_{0}\vcentcolon=11+\alpha k
, we get, for any
l\leq 8
, for any

0\leq\gamma\leq s-s_{0}
,

\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\right|\leq C_{k}^{N+1}\left\|f\right\|_{k,s_{0}}^{N}\left\|f\right\|_{k,s}\,,\quad\forall x,z\in\mathbb{T}\,,

which, recalling
Definition

2.26
, proves the claim in (
4.40
).

Equations

4.39
and
4.40

thus prove (
4.22
).

[Proof of
(
4.23
)-(
4.3
)]
In view of (
4.38
),
in order to expand
\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)

as in (

),
we perform a Taylor expansion
in

of the functions
\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)
and

,

.
By (

) we have

\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j}}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)=\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},0}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)+\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},1}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)\sin z+\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},2}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}+\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(0;x,z\right)

(4.45)

with
\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},l}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)
,
\mathsf{j}=1,2,3
,

,
are the constants computed in (

) and

\varrho^{\mathsf{1},3}_{\alpha}\left(0;x,z\right)=\varrho^{\mathsf{2},3}_{\alpha}\left(0;x,z\right)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(0;x,z\right)\in\widetilde{K}\mathcal{F}^{3}_{0}

(4.46)

is
x
-independent.
Then,
for any

, we expand

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)z^{l}+{\mathsf{R}}^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)

(4.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\sin z+\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,2}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}+\varrho^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right),

where, for

,

\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\ \frac{1}{l!}\left.\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\right|_{z=0}

(4.48)

and

\displaystyle\varrho^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathsf{R}}^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)+\tilde{\mathsf{R}}^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)

(4.49)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\vartheta\right)^{2}\partial_{z}^{3}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,\vartheta z\right)\textnormal{d}\vartheta\ z^{3}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{R}}^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\left(z-\sin z\right)+\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,2}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\left(z^{2}-\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}\right).

Notice that

is
2\pi
-periodic thanks to (

).
In view of (
4.45
) and (
4.47
),
we obtain the expansion (

)
with, for any

,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\ {\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{j},l}_{\alpha}\left(0;x\right)}+\sum_{p=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\,,\qquad l=0,1,2\,,

(4.50)

\displaystyle\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\vcentcolon={\varrho^{\mathsf{j},3}_{\alpha}\left(0;x,z\right)}+\sum_{p=1}^{N}\varrho^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)+\varrho^{\mathsf{j},>N,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\,,

and

\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},>N,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\ \sum_{p>N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\,,\qquad\varrho^{\mathsf{j},>N,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\vcentcolon=\ \sum_{p>N}\varrho^{\mathsf{j},p,3}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\,.

(4.51)

Let us prove (
4.24
).
We deduce that each

\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)=\frac{1}{l!}\partial_{z}^{l}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathsf{j},p,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,0\right)
,

Analogously the non-homogenous term
