4.3
Paralinearization of the quasilinear integral term
\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)

In this section we paralinearize
\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)
.

Lemma 4.4
.

The term
\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)
defined in (
4.13
) can be written as

\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[-\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+{\rm i}S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)+V\left[\mathcal{I}\right]\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

(4.52)

where

•

\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)
is the real function

\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\ -\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right];

(4.53)

•

L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\vcentcolon=\ \mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}+\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is a real Fourier multiplier in

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\max\{0,\alpha-1\}}_{0}
(the Fourier multipliers
\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
and
\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)

are defined in
Lemma

3.1
);

•

S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\ -\frac{1}{2}\left(\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)_{x}\ \partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\ r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\xi
is a real symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{\alpha-2}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

V\left[\mathcal{I}\right]\left(f;x\right)
is a function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
2.20
);

•

R\left(f\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

The rest of this section is devoted to prove
Lemma

4.4
.

By
Lemma

2.22
we have

\frac{\delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-2}\right]\delta_{z}f+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\delta_{z}f\right]\left(r^{-2}-1\right)+R_{1}\left(r^{-2}-1\right)\delta_{z}f+R_{2}\left(\delta_{z}f\right)\left(r^{-2}-1\right)

with smoothing operators
R_{1},R_{2}
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{1}
for any
\rho\geq 0
.
Hence, recalling the definition of
\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)
in (
4.13
), we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathsf{j}}=1}^{4}\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{j}}\left(f\right)\,,

(4.54)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-2}\right]\frac{\delta_{z}f}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\delta_{z}f\right]\frac{1}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{-2}-1\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]R_{1}\left(r^{-2}-1\right)\frac{\delta_{z}f}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]R_{2}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}f}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{-2}-1\right)\,.

\displaystyle\ +\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)+r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\,\sin z+\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,2}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\right)\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\delta_{z}f}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha-2}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}f\left(x\right)+\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)f\,.

(4.62)

By (

),

, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}\left(f\right)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right]\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f

(4.63)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)\ \partial_{\xi}\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

where
P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

In order to compute
\mathcal{I}_{1,2}\left(f\right)
in (
4.60
)
we need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

We have

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\delta_{z}\phi\ \textnormal{d}z={\rm i}\ \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)D\ \phi,

(4.64)

where
\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is defined in (
3.6
).

Proof.

By oddness

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}z=0
and thus, integrating by parts,

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\delta_{z}\phi\ \textnormal{d}z

\displaystyle=-\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\sin z}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\alpha/2}}\phi\left(x-z\right)\ \textnormal{d}z=-\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)\phi\left(x-z\right)\,\textnormal{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\frac{\phi^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)}{\left[2\left(1-\cos z\right)\right]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-1}}\ \textnormal{d}z={\rm i}\ \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)D\ \phi

using (
3.20
). This proves (
4.64
).
∎

Lemma

4.5
and
Proposition

2.21
and since

\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is a symbol of order
\alpha-3

gives that

\mathcal{I}_{1,2}\left(f\right)=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}\ r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\xi+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

(4.65)

where
P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
2.20
).

(4.67)

is a function in

,

\mathcal{I}_{1,5}\left(f\right)=R\left(f\right)\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f+\tilde{R}\left(f\right)f\,,\qquad R\left(f\right),\tilde{R}\left(f\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,.

+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\partial_{x}\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+1\right)\ \partial_{\xi}\mathsf{T}_{\alpha}^{1}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+{\rm i}\ r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\xi\right]f\\

4.4
Paralinearization of the quasilinear integral term
\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)

In this section we paralinearize
\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

The term
\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)
defined in (
4.15
) can be written as

\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[-\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+{\rm i}\ S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)+V\left[\mathcal{J}\right]\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

(4.80)

where

•

\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)
is the real function

\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\ \left(\mathsf{K}^{2,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)-1\right)+\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\frac{f^{\prime}(x)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}^{3,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\ \in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right];

(4.81)

•

L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\vcentcolon=-\left|\xi\right|^{2}\ \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is a real Fourier multiplier in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\alpha-1}_{0}

(the Fourier multiplier
\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)

is defined in
Lemma

3.1
);

•

S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=-\frac{\partial_{x}}{2}\left(\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\left(\left(\alpha-2\right)\mathsf{K}^{2,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(f^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{3,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)-f^{\prime\prime}\mathsf{K}^{3,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\right)\right)\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\xi
is a real symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{\alpha-2}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

V\left[\mathcal{J}\right]\left(f;x\right)
is a real function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
2.20
);

•

R\left(f\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of
Lemma

4.6
.

By

2.22
we obtain that

\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)=\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\right]\ \left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)\\
+R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)+R_{2}\left(f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\right)\left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)

(4.82)

where

are smoothing operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{1}
.
Hence, recalling the definition of

), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{j}=1}^{4}\mathcal{J}_{\mathsf{j}}\left(f\right)\,,

(4.83)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)\right]f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\right]\ \left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{4}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits R_{2}\left(f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\right)\left(\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}f}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z\,.

Step 1

(Paralinearization of
\mathcal{J}_{1}

)

(4.84)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1,1}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1,3}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits{\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\varrho^{\left[3-\alpha\right]}_{\alpha}\left(f;x,z\right)\right]f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)}\ \textnormal{d}z\,,

where, by

(4.85)

Now, by (

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits f^{\prime}\left(x-z\right)\frac{\sin z}{\left|2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right|^{\alpha}}\ \textnormal{d}z=\left|D\right|^{2}\ \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)f

(4.86)

and, using Proposition
2.21
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1,1}\left(f\right)

\displaystyle=\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(\mathsf{K}^{2,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)-1\right)\right]\left|D\right|^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|D\right|\right)f

(4.87)

\displaystyle=\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{K}^{2,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\right]{\rm i}(\alpha-2)M_{\alpha}(|D|)Df+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]\partial_{x}f

+{\rm i}\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\frac{\partial_{x}}{2}\left(\nu_{2}\left(f;x\right)\right)\ \partial_{\xi}\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right)+\left(\alpha-2\right)\mathsf{K}^{2,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\ \mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\xi+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f\,.

4.5
Proof of
Theorem

4.1

We now paralinearize the scalar field in
Equation

4.20
.
We apply
Lemma

2.22

\displaystyle r^{2-\alpha}\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)\right]\left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)+R_{1}\left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+R_{2}\left(\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)\right)\left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right),

\displaystyle r^{-\alpha}\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\right]\left(r^{-\alpha}-1\right)+R_{1}\left(r^{-\alpha}-1\right)\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)+R_{2}\left(\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\right)\left(r^{-\alpha}-1\right).

We thus apply (
4.73
),
Lemmas

2.19
,
4.4
,
4.6
,
2.21
and
2.23

and obtain that there exist real functions
\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{I}},\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{J}}
in

\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
such that

\displaystyle r^{2-\alpha}\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f,

(4.106)

\displaystyle r^{-\alpha}\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f,

for some smoothing operator
R\left(f\right)
in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

A key fact proved in the next lemma is that the imaginary part of the symbol in (
4.107
) has order at most
-1
. This is actually an
effect of the linear Hamiltonian structure, see Remark
4.8
.

Lemma 4.7
.

It results

\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=\ -\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\left(1+\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f\\
\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[V_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)+V_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

(4.107)

where

•

L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
and
L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
are the real Fourier multipliers defined in
Lemmas

4.4
and
4.6
;

•

\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)
,

\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)
,
V_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)
,

V_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)
are real functions
in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

R\left(f\right)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

Propositions

2.21
,
4.4
and
4.6

give that

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\left(-\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+{\rm i}\ S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right)\right]f

\displaystyle\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\left(r^{2-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f

\displaystyle\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[V_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f,

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)=

\displaystyle\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\left(-\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+{\rm i}\ S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right)\right]f

\displaystyle\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\left(r^{-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f

\displaystyle\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[V_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f,

so that, defining

\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=r^{2-\alpha}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)-1
and

\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=r^{-\alpha}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)-1
, we get

\displaystyle\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)

\displaystyle+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\left(1+\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f

\displaystyle\ +{\rm i}\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)+r^{-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f

(4.108a)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\ \textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(r^{2-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\left(r^{-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f

(4.108b)

\displaystyle\ +\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[V_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)+V_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f\,.

We now prove that the sum of (
4.108a
) and (
4.108b
)
give a paradifferential term of order
-1
.
We first note that,
by
Lemma

3.7
, we have the
asymptotic expansions

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\xi\right|^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=\ \breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}+m_{\alpha-3}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),

\displaystyle\xi\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=\ \breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+m_{\alpha-4}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),

(4.109)

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}+\tilde{\mathbb{V}}_{\alpha}+m_{\alpha-3}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\breve{c}_{\alpha}\vcentcolon=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\,,

so that

\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+m_{\alpha-4}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),\qquad\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=-\left(\alpha-1\right)\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+m_{\alpha-4}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\,.

By the explicit definition of the symbols
S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}
and
S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}
in
Lemmas

4.4
and
4.6
and (
4.109
)
we have the expansion of the symbol in (
4.108a
)

\displaystyle{\rm i}\left(r^{2-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)+r^{-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right)

(4.110)

\displaystyle={\rm i}\left[-r^{2-\alpha}\ \frac{1}{2}\left(\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\right)_{x}\left(f;x\right)+\left(\alpha-1\right)r^{-\alpha}\ \frac{1}{2}\left(\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\right)_{x}\left(f;x\right)+A_{\alpha,1}\left(f;x\right)\right]\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+{\rm i}P\left(f;x,\xi\right),

where

A_{\alpha,1}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\left(\alpha-2\right)\mathsf{K}^{2,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(f^{\prime}\ \mathsf{K}^{3,1}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)-f^{\prime\prime}\ \mathsf{K}^{3,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\right)\right]

(4.111)

is a function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, recalling (
4.3
).
Then the sum of (
4.108a
) and (
4.108b
) gives

r^{2-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{I},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)+r^{-\alpha}\ S_{\mathcal{J},\alpha-2}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\\
-\frac{1}{2}\left[\Big.\left(r^{2-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\ \left(r^{-\alpha}\right)_{x}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\partial_{\xi}L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]\\
=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\left[-r^{2-\alpha}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\right)+\left(\alpha-1\right)r^{-\alpha}\left(1+\nu_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)\right]_{x}}_{=\left(A_{\alpha,0}\left(f;x\right)\right)_{x}}+A_{\alpha,1}\left(f;x\right)\right\}\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+P\left(f;x,\xi\right),

(4.112)

where, having
substituting the explicit values of
\nu_{\mathcal{I}}
,
\nu_{\mathcal{J}}
in (
4.53
), (
4.81
),
we define

A_{\alpha,0}\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}\left[\mathsf{K}^{1,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\left(\alpha-1\right)\mathsf{K}^{2,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)+\frac{f^{\prime}}{r^{2}}\ \mathsf{K}^{3,0}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)\right]+\left(2-\alpha\right)

(4.113)

which is a function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
We finally write

\eqref{eq:pain3}=\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(A_{\alpha,0}\left(f;x\right)\right)_{x}+A_{\alpha,1}\left(f;x\right)\right]\breve{c}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-3}\xi+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)=P\left(f;x,\xi\right)

(4.114)

in view of the key cancellation

A_{\alpha,1}\left(f;x\right)+\frac{1}{2}\ \left(A_{\alpha,0}\left(f;x\right)\right)_{x}=0\,.

(4.115)

proved in
Appendix

A
.
By (
4.114
) we deduce that (
4.108
) has the form
(
4.107
).
∎

Remark 4.8
.

The algebraic
reason of the cancellation (
4.115
)
is that a symbol of the form
{\rm i}g\left(f;x\right)|\xi|^{\alpha-3}\xi
, as in (
4.110
),
with a real function
g\left(f;x\right)
,
does not
respect the Hamiltonianity condition (
2.21
).

The next lemma enables to highlight the quasilinear structure of the vector field in (
4.20
).

Lemma 4.9
.

It results

\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{2-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}\right]\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)\\
=-\left(\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)^{-1}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\nu\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right]f+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tilde{\tilde{V}}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

(4.116)

where
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is the Fourier multiplier defined in
Lemma

3.1
and

•

\nu\left(f;x\right),\tilde{\tilde{V}}\left(f;x\right)
are
real functions in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

P\left(f;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

R\left(f\right)
is
a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\rho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

By (
4.11
) and (
4.73
) we have

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)=2\ \frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}\left(f+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{-\alpha}-1\right]f\right)+R\left(f\right)f.

(4.117)

Notice now, from
Lemmas

4.4
and
4.6
and
Lemma

3.1
, that

L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)-2\ \frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{2}}=\left(\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)^{-1}L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\ .

(4.118)

Now we claim that

\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)L_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)L_{\mathcal{J}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)=\left(\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)^{-1}\nu\left(f;x\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\tilde{V}\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right),

(4.119)

for a suitable real functions
\nu,\tilde{V}
in

\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and a symbol
P
in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
From
Lemmas

4.4
and
4.6

and the asymptotic decomposition of
\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}
and
\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}
in

Lemma

3.7
,
we have that

\displaystyle\text{l.h.s. of \eqref{eq:buuh5}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)\mathsf{T}^{1}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)-\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{2}\mathsf{M}_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}}{\alpha-1}\ \left(\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(f;x\right)-\left(\alpha-1\right)\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\,.

Defining

\nu\left(f;x\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{\tilde{\nu}_{I}\left(f;x\right)-\left(\alpha-1\right)\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{J}}\left(f;x\right)}{2-\alpha}
and using the identity

\Gamma\left(3-\alpha\right)=\left(2-\alpha\right)\Gamma\left(2-\alpha\right)
, we get

\text{l.h.s. of \eqref{eq:buuh5}}=\ \left(\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)^{-1}\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(3-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\left|n\right|^{\alpha-1}}{\alpha-1}\ {\nu}\left(f;x\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\,.

(4.120)

By
Lemma

3.6
we have

\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(3-\alpha\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\frac{\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}}{\alpha-1}\ {\nu}\left(f;x\right)=\nu\left(f;x\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\,.

(4.121)

Finally plugging (
4.121
) in (
4.120
) we deduce (
4.119
).

Equations

4.107
,
4.117
,
4.118
and
4.119
give that for suitable
\nu,\tilde{\tilde{V}}\in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
the desired decomposition provided in
Equation

4.116
.
∎

We can finally paralinearize
Equation

4.20
. Using (
4.106
) and (
4.116
) we have

r^{2-\alpha}\left(\mathcal{I}\left(f\right)+R\left(f\right)f\right)+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\left(0\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \left(r^{2-\alpha}-1\right)+r^{-\alpha}\,\mathcal{J}\left(f\right)\\
=-\left(\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}\right)^{-1}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\nu\left(f;x\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]f+R\left(f\right)f

where
V\left(f;x\right)
is a real function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
This, combined with the observation that
\partial_{x}\circ R\left(f\right)\in\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, proves that
Equation

4.20

has the form (
4.1
).

\Box

5
Birkhoff normal form reduction up to cubic terms

In this section we
reduce the equation
(
4.1
)
to its Birkhoff normal form up to a cubic smoothing vector field, from which Theorem

1.1
easily follows.
From now on we consider
\alpha\in(1,2)
.

Proposition 5.1

(Cubic Birkhoff normal form)
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(1,2\right)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
There exists
\underline{\rho}\vcentcolon=\underline{\rho}\left(N,\alpha\right)
, such that for any
\rho\geq\underline{\rho}
there exists
\underline{K^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\underline{K^{\prime}}\left(\rho,\alpha\right)>0
such that for any

K\geq\underline{K^{\prime}}
there is
\underline{s_{0}}>0
such that for any
s\geq\underline{s_{0}}
, there is
\underline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)>0
such that
for any
0<\epsilon_{0}\leq\underline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)
and any solution
f\in B^{K}_{\underline{s_{0}},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)\cap C^{K}_{\ast}\left(I;H^{s}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

of the equation (
4.1
) the following holds:

•

there exists a real
invertible operator
\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)
on
H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})
satisfying the following:
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
there are
C\vcentcolon=C(s,\epsilon_{0},K)
and
\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
,
such that for any

f\in B^{K}_{\underline{s_{0}},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}(s)\right)
and

v\in C^{K-\underline{K^{\prime}}}_{*}\left(I;H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})\right)
,
for any
0\leq k\leq K-\underline{K^{\prime}}
,
t\in I
,

\Big\|\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)v\Big)\Big\|_{s-k}+\Big\|\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)^{-1}v\Big)\Big\|_{s-k}\leq C\|v\|_{k,s}\,;

(5.1)

•

the variable
\mathpzc{y}\vcentcolon=\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)f
solves the equation

\partial_{t}\mathpzc{y}+{\rm i}\ \omega_{\alpha}\left(D\right)\mathpzc{y}+{\rm i}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]\mathpzc{y}=R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)\mathpzc{y}

(5.2)

where

\bullet

\omega_{\alpha}\left(\xi\right)=\xi L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
,
with
L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)

defined in
Lemma

3.1
, is a Fourier multiplier
of order
\alpha
;

\bullet

d\left(f;t,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{\alpha}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
independent of
x
, satisfying (
2.20
),
with
\textnormal{Im}\,d\left(f;t,\xi\right)
in the space
\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

\bullet

R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-(\rho-\underline{\rho}-\alpha)}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

The bounds (
5.1
) imply in particular that for any
s\geq s_{0}
, there exists
C\vcentcolon=C_{s,K,\alpha}>0
such that

C^{-1}\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}\leq\left\|\mathpzc{y}\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}\leq C\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}\,,\quad\forall t\in I\,.

(5.3)

Note that
the
x
-independent symbol
d\left(f;t,\xi\right)
in (
5.2
) has homogeneity
at least
2
by Remark
2.6
.

Reduction to constant coefficients up to a smoothing operator.

The first step is to reduce the symbol of the paradifferential operator in (
4.1
)
to a constant coefficient one, up to a smoothing operator.

Proposition 5.2

(Reduction to constant coefficients up to smoothing operators)
.

Let
\alpha\in\left(1,2\right)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
There exists
\underline{\rho}\vcentcolon=\underline{\rho}\left(N,\alpha\right)
, such that for any
\rho\geq\underline{\rho}
there exists
\underline{K^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\underline{K^{\prime}}\left(\rho,\alpha\right)>0

such that for any

K\geq\underline{K^{\prime}}

there are
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
for any solution
f\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)

of (
4.1
) the following holds:

•

there exists a real
invertible operator
\Psi\left(f;t\right)
on
H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})
satisfying (
5.1
);

•

the variable
\mathpzc{g}\vcentcolon=\Psi\left(f;t\right)f
solves the equation

\partial_{t}\mathpzc{g}+\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]\mathpzc{g}=R\left(f;t\right)\mathpzc{g}

(5.4)

where

\bullet

L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)
is the Fourier multiplier
of order
\alpha-1

defined in
Lemma

3.1
;

\bullet

\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)
is a
x
-independent real function in

\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

\bullet

\mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)

is an
x
-independent symbol
in
\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

satisfying (
2.20
), with

\ \textnormal{Im}\mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)

in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

\bullet

R\left(f;t\right)
is a real smoothing operator in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-(\rho-\underline{\rho})}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

5.2
relies
on general results (given in
Appendix

B
)
that describe how paradifferential operators
are conjugated under the flow generated by a paradifferential operator,
which is Hamiltonian up to zero order operators.
We shall use repeatedly the following result.

Lemma 5.3

(Flows of Hamiltonian operators up to order zero)
.

Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
0\leq K^{\prime}\leq K

and
\delta\geq 0
. Let us consider a
“Hamiltonian operators up to order zero"

\Lambda\left(f,\tau;t\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\lambda\left(f,\tau;t,x,\xi\right)\right]

where
\lambda\left(f,\tau;t,x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\,\Sigma\Gamma^{-\delta}_{K,K^{\prime},p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
uniformly in
\left|\tau\right|\leq 1
, with

\,{\rm Im}\,\lambda\left(f,\tau;t,x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,K^{\prime},p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
2.20
).
Then there exists
s_{0}>0
such that,
for any
f\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
, the equation

\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}\tau}\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)=\Lambda\left(f,\tau;t\right)\ \Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)\,,\qquad\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,0;t\right)=\textnormal{Id}\,\,,

(5.5)

has a unique solution

\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)

satisfying the following properties:
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
the linear map
\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)
is
bounded and invertible on

H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R})
and
there are a constant
C\vcentcolon=C(s,\epsilon_{0},K)

and

\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
such that, for any
f\in B_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}^{K}(I;\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}(s))
,
for any
0\leq k\leq K-K^{\prime}
,
v\in C_{*}^{K-K^{\prime}}(I;H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R}))
,
t\in I
,

\Big\|\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)v\Big)\Big\|_{s-k}+\Big\|\partial_{t}^{k}\Big(\Phi_{\Lambda}\left(f,\tau;t\right)^{-1}v\Big)\Big\|_{s-k}\leq C\|v\|_{k,s}

(5.6)

uniformly in
|\tau|\leq 1
.

Proof.

Since the imaginary part of the symbol
\lambda
has order
-1
,
the flow
\Phi_{\Lambda}
of (
5.5
)
is well-posed and satisfies
(
5.6
) arguing
as in
[
5
, Lemma 3.22]
.
Moreover it preserves the subspace of real functions since

\lambda\left(f,\tau;t,x,\xi\right)
satisfies (
2.20
).
∎

In the proof of Proposition
5.2

it is convenient to preserve the linear
Hamiltonian structure of (
4.1
) up to order zero along the reduction
which leads to (
5.4
), since it
guarantees that the symbol
\left(1+\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)
, as well as those obtained in the intermediate reduction steps, are real, at least up to order
-1
.

Reduction to constant coefficients at principal order.

We first reduce to constant coefficients the highest order
paradifferential operator in (
4.1
).
We conjugate (
4.1
) via the transformation

f^{\left[1\right]}\vcentcolon=\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)f

(5.7)

where
\Phi_{B}\left(f,\tau\right)
is the flow
generated as in
Lemma

5.3

by the Hamiltonian operator

B\left(f,\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(f,\tau;x\right)\right]\,,\qquad b\left(f,\tau;x\right)\vcentcolon=\frac{\beta\left(f;x\right)}{1+\tau\ \partial_{x}\left(\beta\left(f;x\right)\right)}\,,

(5.8)

where
\beta\left(f;x\right)
is a real function to be chosen.

Lemma 5.4

(Reduction to constant coefficients at principal order)
.

Let

\beta\left(f;x\right)\in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

be the periodic function
of the diffeomorphism
x\mapsto x+\beta\left(f;x\right)
of
\,{\mathbb{T}}

whose inverse diffeomorphism
is
y\mapsto y+\breve{\beta}\left(f;y\right)
,
where

\displaystyle\breve{\beta}\left(f;y\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{y}^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{1+\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)}{1+\nu\left(f;y\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}-1\right]\in\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=\left(\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\left(1+\nu\left(f;y\right)\right)^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}\textnormal{d}y\right)^{-\alpha}-1\,,

(5.9)

and
\nu\left(f;y\right)
is the real function defined in Theorem

4.1
.
Then, if
f
solves (
4.1
),
the variable

defined

(5.10)

where

•

\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)
is the

-independent function
in

2.20
);

is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\left(\rho-N\right)}_{K,1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

Proof.

If
f

) then,
the variable

),
and
\partial_{t}\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)\circ\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)^{-1}=-\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)\circ\left(\partial_{t}\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)^{-1}\right)
, the equation

\partial_{t}f^{\left[1\right]}+\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left(1+\nu\left(f;x\right)\right)\left(c^{1}_{\alpha}\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}+\mathbb{V}_{\alpha}+m_{\alpha-3}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\right)+V\left(f;x\right)+P\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]\circ\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)^{-1}\ f^{\left[1\right]}\\
+\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)\circ\left(\partial_{t}\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)^{-1}\right)f^{\left[1\right]}=\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)\circ R\left(f\right)\circ\Phi_{B}\left(1\right)^{-1}f^{\left[1\right]}\,.

(5.11)

By (

(5.12)

\displaystyle=\ \partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[c^{1}_{\alpha}\ \left(1+\nu\left(f;y\right)\right)\left(1+\left.\partial_{y}\breve{\beta}\left(f;y\right)\right)^{\alpha}\right|_{y=x+\beta(f;x)}\ \left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}+P_{1}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\right]+R\left(f\right)

where

(5.25)

up to a smoothing operator,
with a symbol

P^{\left[\mathsf{j+1}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,\mathsf{j},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
By
(
5.23
), (
5.24
), (
5.25
)
and since

\Phi_{W^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}}\left(1\right)^{-1}\circ R^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t\right)\circ\Phi_{W^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}}\left(1\right)

is in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\left(\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}}-N\left(1-\mathsf{j}\delta\right)\right)}_{K,\mathsf{j},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
we deduce that (
5.19
) has the form
(
5.17
) with
\mathsf{j}
replaced by
\mathsf{j}+1

where

\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}+1\right]}_{\alpha}\vcentcolon=\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}_{\alpha}+\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits r^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\textnormal{d}x
and

r^{\left[\mathsf{j}+1\right]}\vcentcolon=T^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}+Q_{-\mathsf{j}\delta}
.
∎

Now, implementing an analogous algorithmic procedure for the symbols of
order
\leq-1
,
we reduce the equation (
5.17
) for
\mathsf{j}=\mathsf{j}_{\ast}

to constant coefficients up to a smoothing operator.

Lemma 5.6

(Reduction to constant coefficients up to smoothing operators)
.

For any integer
\mathsf{j}\geq\mathsf{j}_{\ast}
,
for any
K\geq\mathsf{j}
there exist a

•

symbol

\mathpzc{d}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\left(1+\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\right)L_{\alpha}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)+\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)

with
\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,\mathsf{j}-1,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

and

\textnormal{Im}\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)

in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,\mathsf{j}-1,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
independent of
x
and satisfying (
2.20
);

•

symbol
P^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-1-\left(\mathsf{j}-\mathsf{j}_{\ast}\right)\delta}_{K,\mathsf{j},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
2.20
);

•

a real smoothing operator

R^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t\right)
in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\left(\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}\right)}_{K,\mathsf{j},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

bounded linear operators
W^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]
where

w^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=-\partial_{x}^{-1}\left[\frac{P^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)-\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits P^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\textnormal{d}x}{\left(1+\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\right)\ c^{1}_{\alpha}\alpha\left|\xi\right|^{\alpha-1}}\right]\in\Sigma\Gamma^{-1-\left(\mathsf{j}-\mathsf{j}_{\ast}+1\right)\delta}_{K,\mathsf{j},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right];

(5.26)

and
s_{0}>0
, such that
if
f\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
is a solution of (
4.1
) then
f^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\vcentcolon=\prod_{\mathsf{j}^{\prime}=1}^{\mathsf{j}-1}\Phi_{W^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\prime}\right]}}\left(f;1\right)^{-1}\circ\Phi_{B}\left(f;1\right)f
solves

\partial_{t}f^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}+\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathpzc{d}^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,\xi\right)+P^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]f^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}=R^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}\left(f;t\right)f^{\left[\mathsf{j}\right]}.

(5.27)

We now conclude the proof of Proposition
5.2
. Let

\mathsf{j}^{\ast}:=\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\left(\rho\right)\vcentcolon=\min\left\{\mathsf{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\ \middle|\ \left(\mathsf{j}-\mathsf{j}_{\ast}\right)\delta>\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}\right\},

which is explicitly

\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\vcentcolon=\left\lceil\frac{\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}}{\alpha-1}\right\rceil+\mathsf{j}_{\ast}=\left\lceil\frac{\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}}{\alpha-1}\right\rceil+\left\lceil\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\right\rceil+1
,
so that
\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[P^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\left(\rho-\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}\right)}_{K,\mathsf{j}^{\ast},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
by
2.18
.
Then the equation
(
5.27
) with
\mathsf{j}=\mathsf{j}^{*}
has the form
(
5.4
) with

\mathpzc{g}=f^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{*}\right]}=\Psi\left(f;t\right)f\,,\qquad\Psi\left(f;t\right):=\prod_{\mathsf{j}^{\prime}=1}^{\mathsf{j}^{\ast}-1}\Phi_{W^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\prime}\right]}}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}\circ\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)\,,

symbol
\mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{H}^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\right]}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)
,
smoothing operator

R\left(f;t\right)\vcentcolon=R^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\right]}\left(f;t\right)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[P^{\left[\mathsf{j}^{\ast}\right]}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]
,
and defining
\underline{\rho}\left(N,\alpha\right)\vcentcolon=\rho_{\mathsf{j}_{\ast}}
and

\underline{K^{\prime}}\left(\rho,\alpha\right)\vcentcolon=\mathsf{j}^{\ast}
.

\Box

Birkhoff normal form step.

We now perform one step of Birkhoff normal form to cancel out
the quadratic term in (
5.4
) which,
since
c_{0}\left(f\right)
and
H_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)
vanish quadratically at
f=0
,
comes only from
R\left(f;t\right)\mathpzc{g}
.

By Proposition
5.1
and
using Proposition
2.21
we first rewrite
(
5.4
) as

\partial_{t}\mathpzc{g}+{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}\left(D\right)\mathpzc{g}+{\rm i}\,\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]\,\mathpzc{g}=R_{1}\left(f\right)\mathpzc{g}+R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)\mathpzc{g}

(5.28)

where

i)

d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\vcentcolon={\mathpzc{c}_{0}\left(f\right)\omega_{\alpha}\left(\xi\right)+\xi\ \mathsf{H}_{\alpha}\left(f;t,\xi\right)}
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{\alpha}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
independent of
x
,
with imaginary part

\textnormal{Im}\,d\left(f;t,\xi\right)
in
\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

ii)

R_{1}\left(f\right)
is a real homogenous smoothing operator
in
\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{\ -(\rho-\underline{\rho})}_{1}
, that we expand
(cf. (
2.22
)) as

R_{1}\left(f\right)v=\sum_{n,k,j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\},\atop n+j=k}\left(r_{1}\right)_{n,j,k}f_{n}v_{j}e^{{\rm i}kx}\,,\quad\quad\left(r_{1}\right)_{n,j,k}\in\mathbb{C}\,,

(5.29)

and
R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-(\rho-\underline{\rho})}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

In order to remove
R_{1}\left(f\right)

we conjugate (
5.28
) with the flow

\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)=Q\left(f\right)\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)\,,\qquad\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{0}\left(f\right)={\rm Id}\,,

(5.30)

generated by the
1
-homogenous smoothing operator

Q\left(f\right)v=\sum_{n,k,j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\},\atop n+j=k}q_{n,j,k}f_{n}v_{j}e^{{\rm i}kx}\,,\quad q_{n,j,k}\vcentcolon=\frac{-(r_{1})_{n,j,k}}{{\rm i}\big(\omega_{\alpha}(k)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)-\omega_{\alpha}(n)\big)}\,,

(5.31)

which is well-defined
by Lemma
3.5
. Note also
that by (
3.30
) and
since (cf. (
2.24
), (
2.23
))

\overline{(r_{1})_{n,j,k}}=(r_{1})_{-n,-j,-k}\,,\qquad|(r_{1})_{n,j,k}|\leq C\frac{{\max}_{2}\left(|n|,|j|\right)^{\mu}}{\max\left(|n|,|j|\right)^{\rho-\underline{\rho}}}\,,

(5.32)

also
Q\left(f\right)
is a real smoothing operator in

\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}_{1}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}}
as
R_{1}\left(f\right)
.

Lemma 5.7

(Birkhoff step)
.

If
\mathpzc{g}
solves (
5.28
)
then the variable

\mathpzc{y}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\mathpzc{g}

solves the equation (
5.2
).

Proof.

To conjugate (
5.28
) we apply a Lie expansion
(similarly to
B.2
). We have

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\,\omega_{\alpha}(D)\,\big(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=-{\rm i}\,\omega_{\alpha}(D)\,+\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,-{\rm i}\,\omega_{\alpha}(D)\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle\ +\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,-{\rm i}\,\omega_{\alpha}(D)\right\rrbracket\right\rrbracket\left(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)\right)^{-1}d\tau\,.

(5.33)

Using that
Q\left(f\right)
belongs to

\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}_{1}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}}

the term in (
5.33
)
is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}_{K,0,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Similarly we obtain

-{\rm i}\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]\left(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\right)^{-1}=-{\rm i}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]

(5.34)

up to a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}_{K,K_{0}^{\prime},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, and

\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\big(R_{1}\left(f\right)+R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)\big)\left(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\right)^{-1}=R_{1}\left(f\right)

(5.35)

plus a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}_{K,K_{0}^{\prime},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Next we consider the contribution coming from the conjugation of
\partial_{t}
.
By a Lie expansion (similarly to

B.2
) we get

\partial_{t}\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\left(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{1}\left(f\right)\right)^{-1}=\partial_{t}Q\left(f\right)\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,\partial_{t}Q\left(f\right)\right\rrbracket+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\!\!(1-\tau)^{2}\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,\partial_{t}Q\left(f\right)\right\rrbracket\right\rrbracket\left(\mathcal{\Phi}_{Q}^{\tau}\left(f\right)\right)^{-1}d\tau\,.

(5.36)

Since the
Eq.

4.1
can be written as

\partial_{t}f=-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f+M\left(f\right)f

where
M\left(f\right)
is a real
\alpha
-operator
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{M}}^{\alpha}_{K,0,1}
by
2.18
and
2.11
, we deduce by Proposition
2.23
that

\partial_{t}Q\left(f\right)=Q\left(-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f+M\left(f\right)f\right)=Q\left(-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f\right)

(5.37)

up to a smoothing operator
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}_{K,0,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Since
Q\left(-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f\right)
is in

\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}_{1}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}

we have that the line (
5.36
) belongs to

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+\underline{\rho}+\alpha}_{K,0,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

We now prove that
Q\left(f\right)
solves
the homological equation

Q\left(-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f\right)+\left\llbracket Q\left(f\right)\,,\,-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)\right\rrbracket+R_{1}\left(f\right)=0\,.

(5.38)

Writing (
5.31
) as

Q\left(f\right)v=\sum_{k,j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\big[Q\left(f\right)\big]_{k}^{j}v_{j}e^{{\rm i}kx}
with
\big[Q\left(f\right)\big]_{k}^{j}\vcentcolon=q_{n,j,k}f_{n}
,
we see that the homological
equation (
5.38
)
amounts to

\left[Q(-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(D)f)\right]^{j}_{k}+\left[Q\left(f\right)\right]^{j}_{k}\big({\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}(k)-{\rm i}\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)\big)+\left[R_{1}\left(f\right)\right]^{j}_{k}=0
,
for any
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
,
and then, recalling (
5.29
),
to

q_{n,j,k}\,{\rm i}\big(\omega_{\alpha}(k)-\omega_{\alpha}\left(j\right)-\omega_{\alpha}(n)\big)+(r_{1})_{n,j,k}=0
.
This proves (
5.38
).

In conclusion, by (
5.33
), (
5.34
), (
5.35
), (
5.36
), (
5.37
)
and (
5.38
) we deduce (
5.2
)
(after renaming
\rho
). The bound (
5.3
)
follows by standard theory of Banach space ODEs for the flow (
5.30
)
and (
5.3
).
∎

In view of Lemma
5.7
,
Proposition
5.1
follows
defining
\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)\vcentcolon=\Phi^{1}_{Q}\left(f\right)\circ\Psi\left(f;t\right)
where
\Psi\left(f;t\right)
is defined in
5.2
and
\Phi^{1}_{Q}\left(f\right)
is defined in (
5.31
). We now easily deduce Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

The following result, analogous to
[
10
, Lemma 8.2]
,
enables to control the time derivatives
\|\partial_{t}^{k}f(t)\|_{s-k\alpha}
of a solution

f(t)
of (
4.1
) via
\|f(t)\|_{s}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
. There exists
s_{0}>0
such that for any
s\geq s_{0}
, any
\epsilon\in\left(0,\overline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)\right)
small, if
f
belongs to

B^{0}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon\right)\cap C^{0}_{\ast}\left(I;H^{s}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

and solves (
4.1
) then
f\in C^{K}_{\ast}\left(I;H^{s}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)\right)
and there exists
C_{1}\vcentcolon=C_{1}\left(s,\alpha,K\right)\geq 1
such that

\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}\leq\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{K,s}\leq C_{1}\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}
for any
t\in I
.

The first step is to choose the parameters in
5.1
.
Let
N\vcentcolon=1
.
In the statement of

5.1
we fix

\rho\vcentcolon=\underline{\rho}\left(1,\alpha\right)+\alpha
and
K\vcentcolon=\underline{K^{\prime}}\left(\rho,\alpha\right)
. Then

5.1
gives us
\underline{s_{0}}>0
. For any
s\geq\underline{s_{0}}
we fix
0<\epsilon_{0}\leq\min\{\overline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right),\underline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)\}
where
\underline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)
is
defined in
5.1
and
\overline{\epsilon_{0}}\left(s\right)
in
Lemma

5.8
.

The key corollary of
5.1
is the following energy estimate
where by the time-reversibility of
\alpha
-SQG we may restrict to positive times
t>0
.

Lemma 5.9

(Quartic energy estimate)
.

Let
f(t)
be a solution of
equation (
4.1
) in

B^{K}_{\underline{s_{0}},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)\cap C^{K}_{\ast}\left(I;H^{s}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)\right)
.
Then there exists
\bar{C}_{2}\left(s,\alpha\right)>1
such that

\left\|f\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}^{2}\leq\bar{C}_{2}\left(s,\alpha\right)\left(\left\|f\left(0\right)\right\|_{s}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|f\left(\tau\right)\right\|_{s}^{4}\textnormal{d}\tau\right)\,,\quad\forall 0<t<T\,.

(5.39)

Proof.

The variable
\mathpzc{y}\vcentcolon=\underline{\Psi}\left(f;t\right)f
defined in
Proposition
5.1
solves the equation (
5.2
)
where
\textnormal{Im}\,d\left(f;t,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]
and, being
x
-independent,

\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[d\left(f;t,\xi\right)\right]
commutes with
\left\langle D\right\rangle^{s}
. Furthermore,
for the above choice of
\rho

it results that
R_{\geq 2}\left(f;t\right)
is in

\mathcal{R}^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]
.
Then by (
2.25
), Lemmata
2.13

and
5.8
we deduce

\left\|\mathpzc{y}\left(t\right)\right\|_{s}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathpzc{y}\left(0\right)\right\|_{s}^{2}+\bar{C}_{1}(s,\alpha)\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\mathpzc{y}\left(\tau\right)\right\|_{s}^{4}\,\textnormal{d}\tau\,,\quad\forall 0<t<T\,,

and, by (
5.3
), we deduce (
5.39
).
∎

The energy estimate (
5.39
), (
1.10
) and
the local existence result in
[
21
]

(which amounts to a local existence result
for the equation (
4.1
)),
imply, by a standard
bootstrap argument,
Theorem

1.1
.

\Box
