Here we comment the lines of code above.

1,2

Several variables are defined, so that

\left(\texttt{x,X,z,a}\right)=\left(x,\mathsf{x},z,\alpha\right)

accordingly to the notation of the present manuscript. The variable
a
, which is the parameter
\alpha
, is limited to the range
\left(0,2\right)
.

3,4

We define
f
as an implicit function depending on the variable
x
only, next we define
Deltaf
as the periodic finite difference
\Delta_{z}f
defined in (
4.2
).

5-7

The function
G1
is the function
\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}
defined in (
4.6
), the function
DXG1
is the function
\left(\mathsf{G}^{1}_{\alpha,z}\right)^{\prime}
defined in (
4.18
) and finally we define
K1
as the function
\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}
defined in
Eq.

4.14
.

8-11

We perform the same computations as
\mathsf{K}^{1}_{\alpha,z}
for the kernels
\mathsf{K}^{2}_{\alpha,z}
,
\mathsf{K}^{3}_{\alpha,z}
defined in
Eqs.

4.16
and
4.21
.

12-14

The asymptotic expansion in
Eq.

4.23
is computed for the three kernels.

15-20

We ask the computer to extract the coefficients of the expansions in
Eq.

4.23
so that

\texttt{C}jl\texttt{(x,a)}=\mathsf{K}^{j,l}_{\alpha}\left(f;x\right)
, for any

j=1,2,3,\ l=0,1
.

21,22

We define the functions
A0
and
A1
as in
Eqs.

4.113
and
4.111
.

23

The last line, line 23, is a statement of truth, which asks the computer whether using algebraic simplifications it can prove that
Eq.

4.115
is true.

Appendix B
Conjugation of paradifferential operators under flows

The main results of this section concern
transformation rules of
paradifferential operators of the form

\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]

under the flow generated by
paradifferential operators which are Hamiltonian,
or Hamiltonian up to order zero.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{N},\ K^{\prime}\leq K,\ N\in\mathbb{N}
with
q\leq N
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
\rho\gg N
. Let

\beta\left(f;t,x\right)
be a function in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and

\Phi_{B}\left(f,\tau\right)
be the flow generated by the Hamiltonian
operator
B\left(f,\tau\right)
defined in (
5.8
).

i

(Conjugation of a paradifferential operator)

Let
\mathsf{a}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
be a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Then

\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]\circ\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a_{0}\left(f,1;t,x,\xi\right)+P\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]+R\left(f;t\right)

(B.1)

where

a_{0}\left(f,\tau;t,x,\xi\right)\vcentcolon=\left.\left(1+\partial_{y}\breve{\beta}\left(f,\tau;t,y\right)\right)\ \mathsf{a}\left(f;\,t,\,y,\,\xi\left(1+\partial_{y}\breve{\beta}\left(f,\tau;t,y\right)\right)\Big.\right)\right|_{y=x+\tau\beta\left(f;t,x\right)}

(B.2)

is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,

P\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
R\left(f;t\right)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m+1+N}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

ii

(Conjugation of
\partial_{t}
)

There exists a function

V\left(f;t,x\right)
in
\Sigma\mathcal{F}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and a smoothing operator

R\left(f;t\right)
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

such that

\displaystyle\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)\circ\left(\partial_{t}\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[V\left(f;t,x\right)\right]+R\left(f;t\right).

(B.3)

iii

(Conjugation of a smoothing operator)

If
\,R\left(f;t\right)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
then
the composed operator

\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)\circ R\left(f;t\right)\circ\Phi_{B}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}
is in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+N}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

We also prove an analogous result when the paradifferential operator which generates the flow has order strictly less than
1
.

Proposition B.2

(Lie expansions)
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{N},\ K^{\prime}\leq K,\ N\in\mathbb{N}
with

q\leq N
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
\rho\gg N
.
Given a symbol
w:=w\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
satisfying

w\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{-{\mathtt{d}}}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,\ {\mathtt{d}}>0\,,\quad\textnormal{Im}w\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\in\Gamma^{-\max\{1,\mathtt{d}\}}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,

(B.4)

and (
2.20
)
and denote
\Phi_{W}\left(f,\tau\right)
the flow generated by

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\Phi_{W}\left(f,\tau\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w(f;t,x,\xi)\right]\ \Phi_{W}\left(f,\tau\right)\,,\qquad\Phi_{W}\left(0\right)=\textnormal{Id}\,.

(B.5)

i

(Conjugation of a paradifferential operator)

Let
\mathsf{a}:=\mathsf{a}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
be a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Then

\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]\circ\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)=\\
\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]-\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right\rrbracket+\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[P\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]+R\left(f;t\right)

(B.6)

where

P\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)
is a
symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2{\mathtt{d}}}_{K,K^{\prime},q+2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
and
R\left(f;t\right)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},{q+2}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
If
\mathsf{a},w
are real and even in
\xi
then

\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right\rrbracket
is Hamiltonian and
P
is real and even in
\xi
.

ii

(Conjugation of
\partial_{t}
)

There exists a symbol
T\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)

in

\Sigma\Gamma^{-{\mathtt{d}}}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

satisfying (
2.20
), and a smoothing operator

R\left(f;t\right)
in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
such that

-\partial_{t}\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}\circ\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[T\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]+R\left(f;t\right)\,.

(B.7)

If
w
is real and even in
\xi
then

\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[T\left(f;t,x,\xi\right)\right]

is Hamiltonian, i.e.
T
is real and even in
\xi
.

iii

(Conjugation of a smoothing operator)

If
\,R\left(f;t\right)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
then
the composed operator

\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)\circ R\left(f;t\right)\circ\Phi_{W}\left(f,1\right)^{-1}
is in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+N\max\{0,\left(1-{\mathtt{d}}\right)\}}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Propositions
B.1

and
B.2
.

Proof of Proposition
B.1

The proof of Propositions
B.1

is inspired by the Egorov type analysis in
[
5
, Section 3.5]
.
The difference is that we highlight the Hamiltonian
structure in (
B.1
) and (
B.3
) of the conjugated operators.

For simplicity we avoid to track the dependence of
\beta,b
and
\Phi_{B}

on the variable
f
, as well as on
t
, and denote

\beta_{x}\left(x\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\left(\beta\left(f;t,x\right)\right)
,

b_{x}\left(\tau;t,x\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\left(b\left(f,\tau;t,x\right)\right)

and
\Phi_{B}(\tau)\vcentcolon=\Phi_{B}(f,\tau)
.
In the sequel
\partial_{x}^{-1}
is the Fourier multiplier with symbol
\left({\rm i}\xi\right)^{-1}
that maps
H^{s}_{0}
onto
H^{s+1}_{0}
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof of item
i
:
conjugation of a paradifferential operator

The conjugated operator

\mathcal{P}\left(\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\circ\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\in\mathcal{L}\left(H^{s}_{0};H^{s-1-m}_{0}\right)\,,\quad\forall s\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(B.8)

satisfies

\mathcal{P}\left(0\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]
, and using that

\partial_{\tau}\left(\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\right)=-\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{\tau}\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)\circ\Phi_{B}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}
, it
solves the Heisenberg equation

\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{P}\left(\tau\right)=\left\llbracket B(\tau)\,,\,\mathcal{P}\left(\tau\right)\right\rrbracket\,,\qquad\mathcal{P}\left(0\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\,.

(B.9)

Lemma B.3
.

The operator
A\left(\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{P}\left(\tau\right)\in\mathcal{L}\left(H^{s}_{0};H^{s-m}_{0}\right)
solves

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\begin{multlined}\partial_{\tau}A\left(\tau\right)={\rm i}\left\llbracket\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi\right]\,,\,A\left(\tau\right)\right\rrbracket-\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]A\left(\tau\right)+A\left(\tau\right)\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]\right)\\
+R^{\prime}\left(\tau\right)\,A\left(\tau\right)-A\left(\tau\right)\,R\left(\tau\right)\end{multlined}\\
&A\left(0\right)=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\end{aligned}\right.

(B.10)

where
R\left(\tau\right)
,
R^{\prime}\left(\tau\right)

are smoothing operators in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},1}
, uniformly
in
\left|\tau\right|\leq 1
,
preserving the zero-average subspaces.

Proof.

By (
5.8
) and
Proposition

2.21
we have

\displaystyle B\left(\tau\right)-\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}\,b\left(\tau;x\right)\,\xi+\tfrac{1}{2}b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]=R\left(\tau\right)

(B.11)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{-1}\circ B({\tau})\circ\partial_{x}=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(\tau;x\right)\right]\circ\partial_{x}=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]+R^{\prime}\left(\tau\right)

(B.12)

where
R\left(\tau\right),R^{\prime}\left(\tau\right)
are smoothing operators in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},1}

preserving the zero-average subspaces.
Then, by (
B.9
), (
B.11
), (
B.12
) we get

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}A(\tau)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}^{-1}\circ B(\tau)\circ\partial_{x}\circ A\left(\tau\right)-A\left(\tau\right)\circ B(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left(\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]\right)A\left(\tau\right)-A\left(\tau\right)\left(\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi+\tfrac{1}{2}b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]\right)+R^{\prime}\left(\tau\right)A\left(\tau\right)-A\left(\tau\right)R\left(\tau\right)

proving (
B.10
).
∎

We now look for an approximate solution of (
B.10
) of the form

A^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)=\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{J}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a_{\mathsf{j}}\left(\tau\right)\right]\,,\qquad a_{0}\left(\tau\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,\quad a_{\mathsf{j}}\left(\tau\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2\mathsf{j}}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,\ \forall\mathsf{j}=1,\ldots,J\,.

(B.13)

We use the following asymptotic expansions
derived by Proposition
2.21

and (
2.28
).

Lemma B.4
.

Let
a
be a symbol
in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. Then the commutator

\left\llbracket\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[{\rm i}b(\tau;x)\xi\right]\,,\,\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a\right]\right\rrbracket=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\left\{b(\tau;x)\xi\big.,\ a\right\}\right]+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r_{-3}(b(\tau),a)\right]+R\left(\tau\right)

with symbols

\left\{b(\tau;x)\xi\big.,\ a\right\}\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,\qquad r_{-3}(b(\tau),a)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\,,

and a smoothing operator

R\left(\tau\right)
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m+1}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}[r,N]
,
uniformly in
\tau
.
Moreover

\tfrac{1}{2}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a\right]\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\right]=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)a+r_{-2}(b(\tau),a)\right]+R\left(\tau\right)

where
r_{-2}(b(\tau),a)
is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, and

R\left(\tau\right)
is a smoothing operator
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

We shall also use the following lemma concerning solutions of a transport equation.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
\,W\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)
by a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
uniformly in
\left|\tau\right|\leq 1
.
Then the unique solution of

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)=\left\{b\left(f,\tau;x\right)\Big.\xi\big.,\ Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)\right\}-b_{x}\left(f,\tau;x\right)Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)+W\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)\\
&\left.Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)\right|_{\tau=0}=Q_{0}\left(f;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\end{aligned}\right.

(B.14)

has the form

\displaystyle Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)

\displaystyle=\left.\left(1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau;\ y\right)\right)Q_{0}\left(f;\ y\ ,\ \xi\left(1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau;\ y\right)\right)\right)\right|_{y=x+\tau\beta\left(f;x\right)}

(B.15)

\displaystyle+\left.\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau;\ y\right)}{1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau^{\prime};\ y\right)}\ W\left(f,\tau^{\prime};\ y+\breve{\beta}\left(f,\tau^{\prime};\ y\right)\ ,\ \frac{\xi\left(1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau;\ y\right)\right)}{1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(f,\tau^{\prime};\ y\right)}\right)\ \textnormal{d}\tau^{\prime}\right|_{y=x+\tau\beta\left(f;x\right)}\,,

which is a symbol in

\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
uniformly in
\left|\tau\right|\leq 1
.

Proof.

The solution

(x(\tau),\xi(\tau))=\phi^{0,\tau}(X,\Xi)
of the characteristics system

\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}\tau}{x}\left(\tau\right)=-b\left(\tau;x\left(\tau\right)\right)\,,\quad\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}\tau}{\xi}\left(\tau\right)=b_{x}\left(\tau;x\left(\tau\right)\right)\ \xi\left(\tau\right)\,,

(B.16)

with initial condition
\left.\left(x\left(\tau\right),\xi\left(\tau\right)\right)\right|_{\tau=0}=\phi^{0,0}(X,\Xi)=\left(X,\Xi\right)
is (cf.
[
5
, p. 83]
)

\left(x\left(\tau\right),\xi\left(\tau\right)\right)=\phi^{0,\tau}\left(X,\Xi\right)=\left(X+\breve{\beta}\left(\tau,X\right),\frac{\Xi}{1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(\tau,X\right)}\right)\,.

(B.17)

By (
B.16
) and (
B.14
) we get

\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}\tau}\left[\xi(\tau)\big.Q\left(\tau;x\left(\tau\right),\xi\left(\tau\right)\right)\right]=\xi(\tau)W\left(\tau;x\left(\tau\right),\xi\left(\tau\right)\right)

and so, by integration,

\xi(\tau)\ Q\left(\tau;x\left(\tau\right),\xi\left(\tau\right)\right)=\Xi Q\left(0;X,\Xi\right)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\xi(\tau^{\prime})W\left(\tau^{\prime};x(\tau^{\prime}),\xi(\tau^{\prime})\right)\textnormal{d}\tau^{\prime}\,.

(B.18)

The inverse flow

\phi^{\tau,0}\left(x,\xi\right)
,
i.e.

(x,\xi)=\phi^{0,\tau}\left(X,\Xi\right)

if and only if
\left(X,\Xi\right)=\phi^{\tau,0}(x,\xi)

is (cf.
[
5
, p. 83]
)

\left(X,\Xi\right)=\phi^{\tau,0}(x,\xi)=\left(x+\tau\beta(x),\xi\left(1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(\tau;y\right)\right)_{|{y=x+\tau\beta(x)}}\right)\,.

(B.19)

In addition, by (
B.17
) and (
B.19
),

\left(x(\tau^{\prime}),\xi(\tau^{\prime})\right)=\phi^{\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\left(x,\xi\right)=\phi^{0,\tau^{\prime}}\left(\phi^{\tau,0}\left(x,\xi\right)\right)=\left.\left(y+\breve{\beta}\left(\tau^{\prime}y\right)\ ,\ \frac{\xi\left(1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(\tau;y\right)\right)}{1+\breve{\beta}_{y}\left(\tau^{\prime};y\right)}\right)\right|_{y=x+\tau\beta\left(x\right)}.

(B.20)

We deduce
(
B.15
)
inserting (
B.19
) and (
B.20
) in (
B.18
).
Finally
Q\left(f,\tau;x,\xi\right)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, by (
B.15
) and Lemmata
2.8
and
2.9
.
∎

Step i): Determination of the principal symbol
a_{0}
.

From (
B.10
), (
B.13
) and Lemma

B.4
the principal symbol
a_{0}
solves the equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}a_{0}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)=\left\{b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi\big.,\ a_{0}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)\right\}-b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\ a_{0}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)\\
&a_{0}\left(0;x,\xi\right)=\mathsf{a}\left(x,\xi\right)\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(B.21)

By
Lemma

B.5
with
W=0
and
Q_{0}=\mathsf{a}
,
the solution of (
B.21
) is given by
(
B.2
).
The operator

A^{(0)}\vcentcolon=A^{(0)}\left(\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a_{0}\left(\tau\right)\right]

solves approximately
(
B.10
)
in the sense that, by (
B.21
)
and
Lemma

B.4
,

\partial_{\tau}A^{(0)}={\rm i}\left\llbracket\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(\tau\right)\xi\right]\,,\,A^{(0)}\right\rrbracket-\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]A^{(0)}-A^{(0)}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]\\
+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{(0)}(\tau)\right]+R^{\left(0\right)}\left(\tau\right)

(B.22)

where
r^{(0)}(\tau)\vcentcolon=-r_{-3}(b,a_{0})-r_{-2}(b,a_{0})
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
R^{\left(0\right)}\left(\tau\right)
is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, unifomly in

\tau\in[0,1]
.

Step ii): Determination of the subprincipal symbol
\sum_{\mathsf{j}=1}^{J}a_{\mathsf{j}}
.

We define
a_{1}(\tau;x,\xi)
as the solution of the
transport equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}a_{1}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)=\left\{b\left(\tau;x\right)\xi\big.,\ a_{1}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)\right\}-b_{x}\left(\tau;x\right)\ a_{1}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)-r^{(0)}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)\\
&a_{1}\left(0;x,\xi\right)=0\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(B.23)

By
Lemma

B.5

the symbol

a_{1}\left(\tau;x,\xi\right)
is in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m-2}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}
.
By
Equations

B.22
,
B.23
and
B.4

A^{(1)}(\tau)\vcentcolon=A^{(0)}(\tau)+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a_{1}(\tau)\right]

is a better approximation of equation (
B.10
) in the sense that

\partial_{\tau}A^{(1)}={\rm i}\left\llbracket\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(\tau\right)\xi\right]\,,\,A^{(1)}\right\rrbracket-\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]A^{(1)}-A^{(1)}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]\\
+\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[r^{(1)}(\tau)\right]+R^{(1)}\left(\tau\right)

(B.24)

where
r^{(1)}\vcentcolon=-r_{-3}(b,a_{1})-r_{-2}(b,a_{1})
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m-4}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
R^{(1)}\left(\tau\right)
are smoothing operators in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
uniformly in
|\tau|\leq 1
.

Repeating
J
times (
J\sim\rho/2
) the above procedure, until the new paradifferential term may be incorporated into the smoothing remainder, we obtain
an operator
A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{J}\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[a_{\mathsf{j}}\left(\tau\right)\right]

as in (
B.13
) solving

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\begin{multlined}\partial_{\tau}A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)={\rm i}\ \left\llbracket\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[b\left(\tau\right)\xi\right]\,,\,A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)\right\rrbracket-\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)-A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\tfrac{b_{x}\left(\tau\right)}{2}\right]+R^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)\end{multlined}\\
&A^{\left(J\right)}\left(0\right)=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\end{aligned}\right.

(B.25)

where
R^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)
are smoothing operators in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
uniformly in

|\tau|\leq 1
.

Step iii) : Analysis of the error.

We finally estimate the difference between the conjugated
operator
P\left(\tau\right)
in
(
B.8
) and

P^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)\vcentcolon=\partial_{x}\circ A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)
.

Lemma B.6
.

P\left(\tau\right)-P^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)

is a smoothing operator
R\left(\tau\right)
in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m+1+N}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

uniformly in
\left|\tau\right|\leq 1
.

Proof.

In view of
Eqs.

B.25
,
B.11
and
B.12
,
the operator

\mathcal{P}^{(J)}(\tau)=\partial_{x}\circ A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)

solves an approximated Heisenberg equation (cf. (
B.9
))

\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{P}^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)=\left\llbracket B\,,\,\mathcal{P}^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)\right\rrbracket+R\left(\tau\right)\,,\qquad R(\tau)\in\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\,.

(B.26)

Recalling (
B.8
) we write

P^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)-P\left(\tau\right)=V(\tau)\Phi_{B}(\tau)^{-1}\quad\text{where}\quad V(\tau)\vcentcolon=P^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)\Phi_{B}(\tau)-\Phi_{B}(\tau)\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\,.

By (
B.26
) we have that

\partial_{\tau}V(\tau)=B(\tau)V(\tau)+R(\tau)\Phi_{B}(\tau)
,
V(0)=0
,
and therefore, by Duhamel and

\partial_{\tau}\Phi_{B}=B\Phi_{B}
we deduce

V(\tau)=\Phi_{B}(\tau)\int_{0}^{\tau}\Phi_{B}(\tau^{\prime})^{-1}R(\tau^{\prime})\Phi_{B}(\tau^{\prime})\,\textnormal{d}\tau^{\prime}

and thus

P^{(J)}\left(\tau\right)-P\left(\tau\right)=\int_{0}^{\tau}\Phi_{B}(\tau)\circ\Phi_{B}(\tau^{\prime})^{-1}\circ R(\tau^{\prime})\circ\Phi_{B}(\tau^{\prime})\circ\Phi_{B}(\tau)^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}\tau^{\prime}\,.

This is a smoothing operator in arguing as in
[
5
, Proof of Thm. 3.27]
.
∎

Lemma

B.6
implies that

P\left(\tau\right)=\partial_{x}\circ A^{\left(J\right)}\left(\tau\right)+R(\tau)

concluding the proof of
B.1
-
i

with symbol
P=\sum_{\mathsf{j}=1}^{J}a_{\mathsf{j}}\left(1\right)
.

Item

ii
follows
similarly as in
[
7
, Lemma A.5]
.
Item

iii

is given in
[
5
, Remark at page 89]
.

Proof of Proposition
B.2

In view of (
B.4
) and
Lemma

5.3
the flow

\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right):=\Phi_{W}\left(f,\tau\right)

generated by (
B.5
) is well posed and

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\left(\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)\right)=-\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right\rrbracket\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)

(B.27)

and a Taylor expansion gives

\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\,\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\circ\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)\\
=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]-\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right\rrbracket+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{\ell}\left(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right)\\
+\frac{\left(-1\right)^{L+1}}{L!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\tau\right)^{L}\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{L+1}\left(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right)\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)\textnormal{d}\tau\,.

(B.28)

Since
\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]
belongs to

\Sigma\Gamma^{-{\mathtt{d}}}_{K,K^{\prime},1}
,
{\mathtt{d}}>0
,
then, by Proposition
2.21

each commutator
\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\cdot\right\rrbracket
gains
{\mathtt{d}}>0

unit of order and one degree of vanishing in
f
and
(
B.28
) is an expansion as in (
B.6
)
in operators with decreasing order and increasing degree of homogeneity
with a symbol
P
of order
m-2{\mathtt{d}}
.
Item
iii
follows as in
[
5
, Remark at page 89]
, see also
[
10
]
, by properties of the flow generated by paradifferential operators.
Thus, Proposition
2.21
,
give that the last term of (
B.28
) belongs to

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{1+m-{\mathtt{d}}\left(L+1\right)+\max\{0,1-{\mathtt{d}}\}N}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
hence if
L+1\geq\frac{\rho+1+m+\max\{0,1-{\mathtt{d}}\}N}{{\mathtt{d}}}

it belongs to
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},q}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
If
w,\mathsf{a}
are real and even in
\xi
, then
the operators
\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]
and
\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]
are Hamiltonian
(cf. (
2.21
)).
The commutator of two Hamiltonian operators

\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}\left(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right)=\partial_{x}\circ S\,,\quad S:=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]-\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,

(B.29)

where
S=S^{*}
,
S=\overline{S}
,
is another Hamiltonian operator where, by

Proposition

2.21
,
the operator
S=\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[s\right]
with
a real
symbol
s
in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m-{\mathtt{d}}}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

even in
\xi
(cf. (
2.21
)),
up to a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime},q+1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, by renaming
\rho
. Applying iteratively this result to

\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{\ell}\left(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[\mathsf{a}\right]\right)
the
formula (
B.6
) follows.

Let us prove (
B.7
). As in (
B.27
) we have that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\left(\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{t}\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)\right)=-\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\left\llbracket\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]\,,\,\partial_{t}\right\rrbracket\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)=\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)

and a Taylor expansion gives

\displaystyle\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{t}\circ\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}+\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-1}}{\ell!}\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{\ell-1}\big(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]\big)

(B.30)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{(-1)^{L}}{L!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\tau\right)^{L}\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)^{-1}\circ\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{L}\left(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]\Big.\right)\circ\Phi_{W}\left(\tau\right)\,\textnormal{d}\tau\,.

Since

\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\circ\partial_{t}\circ\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)=

\partial_{t}+\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\partial_{t}\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)\right)=

\partial_{t}-\left(\partial_{t}\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)

we deduce by (
B.30
) that

-\partial_{t}\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)^{-1}\circ\Phi_{W}\left(1\right)=\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-1}}{\ell!}\textnormal{Ad}_{\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w\right]}^{\ell-1}\big(\partial_{x}\circ\textnormal{Op}^{BW}\left[w_{t}\right]\big)+R

where, if
L\gtrsim_{\mathtt{d},N}\rho
, then
R
is in

\Sigma\dot{\mathcal{R}}^{-\rho}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(renaming
\rho
).
Then (
B.7
) follows arguing as for (
B.6
)
and if
w
is real and even in
\xi
we also deduce that

T
is real and even in
\xi
.
